From Wikipedia, the free encyclopedia

Largest and most populous Canary island

"Teneriffa" redirects here. For the genus of flies, see [Teneriffa spicata](/wiki/Teneriffa_spicata "Teneriffa spicata").

For other uses, see [Tenerife (disambiguation)](/wiki/Tenerife_\(disambiguation\) "Tenerife \(disambiguation\)").

**Tenerife** ([/ˌtɛnəˈriːf(eɪ)/](/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [_TEN -ə-REEF-(ay)_](/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Spanish: [[teneˈɾife]](/wiki/Help:IPA/Spanish "Help:IPA/Spanish") [](//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/e/e2/Pronunciation_of_Tenerife_in_Spanish.ogg/Pronunciation_of_Tenerife_in_Spanish.ogg.mp3 "Play audio")[ⓘ](/wiki/File:Pronunciation_of_Tenerife_in_Spanish.ogg "File:Pronunciation of Tenerife in Spanish.ogg"); formerly spelled _Teneriffe_) is the largest and most-populous [island](/wiki/Island "Island") of the [Canary Islands](/wiki/Canary_Islands "Canary Islands"), an [autonomous community](/wiki/Autonomous_communities_of_Spain "Autonomous communities of Spain") of [Spain](/wiki/Spain "Spain").[5] With a land area of 2,034.38 km2 (785.48 mi2) and a population of 972,018 inhabitants as of January 2026[[update]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit),[6] it is the most-populous island in Spain[5] and the entire [Macaronesia](/wiki/Macaronesia "Macaronesia") region. Tenerife is also home to 42.7% of the total population of the [archipelago](/wiki/Archipelago "Archipelago").[7]

More than seven million tourists (7,384,707 in 2024) visit Tenerife each year, making it by far the most visited island in the archipelago.[8] It is one of the most important tourist destinations in Spain[9] and the world,[10] hosting one of the world's largest [carnivals](/wiki/Carnival "Carnival"), the [Carnival of Santa Cruz de Tenerife](/wiki/Carnival_of_Santa_Cruz_de_Tenerife "Carnival of Santa Cruz de Tenerife"). 

The capital of the island, [Santa Cruz de Tenerife](/wiki/Santa_Cruz_de_Tenerife "Santa Cruz de Tenerife"), is also the seat of the island council (_cabildo insular_). The city and [Las Palmas de Gran Canaria](/wiki/Las_Palmas "Las Palmas") are the co-capitals of the autonomous community of the Canary Islands. The two cities are both home to governmental institutions, such as the offices of the presidency and the ministries. After the [1833 territorial division of Spain](/wiki/1833_territorial_division_of_Spain "1833 territorial division of Spain"), Santa Cruz de Tenerife was the sole capital of the Canary Islands. In 1927, the Crown established the current co-capital arrangement.[11][12] Santa Cruz contains the modern [Auditorio de Tenerife](/wiki/Auditorio_de_Tenerife "Auditorio de Tenerife"), the architectural symbol of the Canary Islands.[13][14]

The island is home to the [University of La Laguna](/wiki/University_of_La_Laguna "University of La Laguna"). Founded in 1792 in [San Cristóbal de La Laguna](/wiki/San_Crist%C3%B3bal_de_La_Laguna "San Cristóbal de La Laguna"), it is the oldest university in the Canaries. The city of La Laguna is a [UNESCO](/wiki/UNESCO "UNESCO") [World Heritage Site](/wiki/World_Heritage_Site "World Heritage Site"). It is the second-most populous city on the island, and the third-most populous in the archipelago. It was the capital of the Canary Islands before Santa Cruz replaced it in 1833.[15] Tenerife is served by two airports: [Tenerife North Airport](/wiki/Tenerife_North_Airport "Tenerife North Airport") and [Tenerife South Airport](/wiki/Tenerife_South_Airport "Tenerife South Airport"). 

[Teide National Park](/wiki/Teide_National_Park "Teide National Park"), located in the center of the island, is also a UNESCO World Heritage Site. It includes [Mount Teide](/wiki/Mount_Teide "Mount Teide"), which has the highest elevation in Spain, and the highest elevation among all the islands in the Atlantic Ocean. It is also the third-largest volcano in the world when measured from its base.[16] Another geographical feature of the island, the [Macizo de Anaga](/wiki/Macizo_de_Anaga "Macizo de Anaga") (massif), has been designated as a UNESCO [Biosphere Reserve](/wiki/Biosphere_Reserve "Biosphere Reserve") since 2015.[17] Tenerife also has the largest number of [endemic](/wiki/Endemic "Endemic") species in Europe.[17]

## Toponymy

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=1 "Edit section: Toponymy")]

[](/wiki/File:Imagen_sint%C3%A9tica_de_Tenerife.jpg)Altitude map of Tenerife, with the highest altitude (Mount [Teide](/wiki/Teide "Teide")) in blue and the lowest (sea level) in black

The name 'Tenerife' likely comes from [Tamazight](/wiki/Amazigh_language "Amazigh language"), but there is no consensus on its correct interpretation.[18]

[](/wiki/File:Tenerife_sentinel_2.jpg)Satellite view (April 2023)

The island's [indigenous people](/wiki/Indigenous_peoples "Indigenous peoples"), the [Guanche](/wiki/Guanches "Guanches") [Berbers](/wiki/Berbers "Berbers"), referred to the island as _Achinet_ or _Chenet_ in [their language](/wiki/Guanche_language "Guanche language") (variant spellings are found in the literature). [Berber](/wiki/Berber_people "Berber people") king [Juba II](/wiki/Juba_II "Juba II") and the [ancient Romans](/wiki/Ancient_Rome "Ancient Rome") called the island of Tenerife _Nivaria_ , from the [Latin](/wiki/Latin "Latin") word _[nix](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/nix#Latin "wikt:nix")_ ([nsg.](/wiki/Nominative_case "Nominative case"); [gsg.](/wiki/Genitive_case "Genitive case") _nivis_ , [npl.](/wiki/Plural "Plural") _nives_), meaning "snow", after the snow-covered peak of the Mount Teide volcano.[19] Later maps dating to the 14th and 15th centuries, drawn by mapmakers such as Bontier and Le Verrier, called the island _Isla del Infierno_ , ("Hell Island"), due to Mount Teide's volcanic eruptions and other volcanic activity. 

Although the name given to the island by the _[Benahoaritas](/wiki/Benahoare "Benahoare")_ (the indigenous peoples of La Palma) was derived from the words _teni_ ("mountain") and _ife_ ("white"),[_[citation needed](/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_] after the island was colonized by the Spanish, the name was modified by Spanish phonology: the letter "r" was added to link the two words, producing the single word Tenerife.[20][21]

Throughout history, though, other explanations for the origin of island's name have been proposed. For example, 17th-century historians [Juan Núñez de la Peña](/wiki/Juan_N%C3%BA%C3%B1ez_de_la_Pe%C3%B1a "Juan Núñez de la Peña") and [Tomás Arias Marín de Cubas](/w/index.php?title=Tom%C3%A1s_Arias_Mar%C3%ADn_de_Cubas&action=edit&redlink=1 "Tomás Arias Marín de Cubas \(page does not exist\)"), among others, suggested that the indigenous peoples might have named the island for the famous Guanche king, [Tinerfe](/wiki/Tinerfe "Tinerfe"), nicknamed "the Great", who ruled Tenerife before the [conquest of the Canary Islands](/wiki/Conquest_of_the_Canary_Islands "Conquest of the Canary Islands") by [Castile](/wiki/Castile_\(historical_region\) "Castile \(historical region\)").[22]

[](/wiki/File:Panorama_Teide_BW.jpg "Panorama of Teide National Park")

[ ](/wiki/File:Panorama_Teide_BW.jpg "File:Panorama Teide BW.jpg")

Panorama of [Teide National Park](/wiki/Teide_National_Park "Teide National Park")

## Demonym

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=2 "Edit section: Demonym")]

The formal [demonym](/wiki/Demonym "Demonym") used to refer to the people of Tenerife is _Tinerfeño/a_ ; also used colloquially is the term _[chicharrero](/wiki/Chicharrero "Chicharrero")/a_.[23] In modern society, the latter term is generally applied only to inhabitants of the capital, Santa Cruz. The term _chicharrero_ was once a [derogatory](/wiki/Derogatory "Derogatory") term used by the people of [La Laguna](/wiki/San_Crist%C3%B3bal_de_La_Laguna "San Cristóbal de La Laguna") when it was the capital, to refer to the poorer inhabitants and fishermen of Santa Cruz. The fishermen typically caught [mackerel](/wiki/Mackerel "Mackerel") and other residents ate [potatoes](/wiki/Potatoes "Potatoes"), assumed to be of low quality by the elite of La Laguna.[23] As Santa Cruz grew in commerce and status, it replaced La Laguna as capital of Tenerife in 1833 during the reign of [Fernando VII](/wiki/Fernando_VII "Fernando VII"). Then, the inhabitants of Santa Cruz used the former insult to identify as residents of the new capital, at La Laguna's expense.[23]

## History

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=3 "Edit section: History")]

[](/wiki/File:MNH_-_Mumie_Mann_1.jpg)[Guanche mummy](/wiki/Guanche_mummies "Guanche mummies") in [Museo de la Naturaleza y el Hombre](/wiki/Museo_de_la_Naturaleza_y_el_Hombre "Museo de la Naturaleza y el Hombre") of [Santa Cruz de Tenerife](/wiki/Santa_Cruz_de_Tenerife "Santa Cruz de Tenerife")

The earliest known human settlement in the islands, dating to around 200 [BC](/wiki/Before_Christ "Before Christ"), was established by [Berbers](/wiki/Berbers "Berbers") known as the [Guanches](/wiki/Guanches "Guanches"),[24] but the [Cave of the Guanches](/wiki/Cave_of_the_Guanches "Cave of the Guanches") in the northern municipality of [Icod de los Vinos](/wiki/Icod_de_los_Vinos "Icod de los Vinos") has provided the oldest chronologies of the Canary Islands, with dates around the sixth century BC.[25]

In terms of technology, the Guanches can be placed among the peoples of the [Stone Age](/wiki/Stone_Age "Stone Age"), although scholars often reject this classification because of its ambiguity. Guanche culture was more advanced culturally, possibly because of Berber cultural features imported from North Africa, but less technologically advanced due to the scarcity of raw materials, especially minerals that would have allowed for the extraction and working of metals. The main activity was gathering food from nature, though fishing and shellfish collection were supplemented with some agricultural practices.[26]

As for religion and [cosmology](/wiki/Cosmology "Cosmology"), the Guanches were [polytheistic](/wiki/Polytheism "Polytheism"), with further widespread belief in an [astral cult](/wiki/Astral_cult "Astral cult"). They also had an [animistic](/wiki/Animism "Animism") religiosity that sacralized certain places, mainly rocks and mountains. Although the Guanches worshiped many gods and ancestral spirits, among the most important were [Achamán](/wiki/Acham%C3%A1n "Achamán") (the god of the sky and supreme creator), [Chaxiraxi](/wiki/Chaxiraxi "Chaxiraxi") (the mother goddess, identified later with the [Virgin of Candelaria](/wiki/Virgin_of_Candelaria "Virgin of Candelaria")), [Magec](/wiki/Magec "Magec") (the god of the sun), and [Guayota](/wiki/Guayota "Guayota") (the demon who is the main cause of evil). Especially significant was the cult of the dead, which practiced the [mummification](/wiki/Mummification "Mummification") of corpses. In addition, small [anthropomorphic](/wiki/Anthropomorphism "Anthropomorphism") and [zoomorphic](/wiki/Zoomorphism "Zoomorphism") stone and clay figurines of the kind typically associated with rituals have been found on the island. Scholars believe they were used as idols, the most prominent of which is the so-called [Idol of Guatimac](/wiki/Guatimac "Guatimac"), which is thought to represent a genius or protective spirit. 

### Territorial organisation before the conquest (the Guanches)

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=4 "Edit section: Territorial organisation before the conquest \(the Guanches\)")]

The title of _[mencey](/wiki/Mencey "Mencey")_ was given to the monarch or king of the Guanches of Tenerife, who governed a _menceyato_ or kingdom. This role was later referred to as a "captainship" by the conquerors. [Tinerfe "the Great"](/wiki/Tinerfe "Tinerfe"), son of the _mencey_ Sunta, governed the island from Adeje in the south. Upon his death, though, his nine children rebelled and argued bitterly about how to divide the island. 

Two independent _achimenceyatos_ were created on the island, and the island was divided into nine _menceyatos_. The _menceyes_ within them formed what were similar to municipalities today.[27] The _menceyatos_ and their _menceyes_ (ordered by the names of descendants of Tinerfe who ruled them) were the following: 

[](/wiki/File:Tenerife_preconquista.png)Territorial map of Tenerife before the conquest

  * [Taoro](/wiki/Menceyato_of_Taoro "Menceyato of Taoro"). _Menceyes_ : Bentinerfe, Inmobach, [Bencomo](/wiki/Bencomo "Bencomo") and [Bentor](/wiki/Bentor "Bentor"). Today it includes [Puerto de la Cruz](/wiki/Puerto_de_la_Cruz "Puerto de la Cruz"), [La Orotava](/wiki/La_Orotava "La Orotava"), [La Victoria de Acentejo](/wiki/La_Victoria_de_Acentejo "La Victoria de Acentejo"), [La Matanza de Acentejo](/wiki/La_Matanza_de_Acentejo "La Matanza de Acentejo"), [Los Realejos](/wiki/Los_Realejos "Los Realejos") and [Santa Úrsula](/wiki/Santa_%C3%9Arsula "Santa Úrsula").
  * [Güímar](/wiki/Menceyato_of_G%C3%BC%C3%ADmar "Menceyato of Güímar"). _Menceyes_ : Acaymo, [Añaterve](/wiki/A%C3%B1aterve "Añaterve") and Guetón. Today this territory is made up of [El Rosario](/wiki/El_Rosario,_Tenerife "El Rosario, Tenerife"), [Candelaria](/wiki/Candelaria,_Tenerife "Candelaria, Tenerife"), [Arafo](/wiki/Arafo "Arafo") and [Güímar](/wiki/G%C3%BC%C3%ADmar "Güímar")
  * [Abona](/wiki/Menceyato_of_Abona "Menceyato of Abona"). _Menceyes_ : Atguaxoña and Adxoña ([Adjona](/wiki/Adjona "Adjona")). Today it includes [Fasnia](/wiki/Fasnia "Fasnia"), [Arico](/wiki/Arico "Arico"), [Granadilla de Abona](/wiki/Granadilla_de_Abona "Granadilla de Abona"), [San Miguel de Abona](/wiki/San_Miguel_de_Abona "San Miguel de Abona") and [Arona](/wiki/Arona,_Tenerife "Arona, Tenerife").
  * [Anaga](/wiki/Menceyato_of_Anaga "Menceyato of Anaga"). _Menceyes_ : [Beneharo](/wiki/Beneharo "Beneharo") and Beneharo II. Today this territory spans the municipalities of [Santa Cruz de Tenerife](/wiki/Santa_Cruz_de_Tenerife "Santa Cruz de Tenerife") and [San Cristóbal de La Laguna](/wiki/San_Crist%C3%B3bal_de_La_Laguna "San Cristóbal de La Laguna").
  * [Tegueste](/wiki/Menceyato_of_Tegueste "Menceyato of Tegueste"). _Menceyes_ : Tegueste, [Tegueste II](/wiki/Tegueste_\(mencey\) "Tegueste \(mencey\)") and Teguaco. Today this territory is made up of [Tegueste](/wiki/Tegueste "Tegueste"), part of the coastal zone of [La Laguna](/wiki/San_Crist%C3%B3bal_de_La_Laguna "San Cristóbal de La Laguna").
  * [Tacoronte](/wiki/Menceyato_of_Tacoronte "Menceyato of Tacoronte"): _Menceyes_ : Rumén and [Acaymo](/wiki/Acaymo "Acaymo"). Today this territory is made up of [Tacoronte](/wiki/Tacoronte "Tacoronte") and [El Sauzal](/wiki/El_Sauzal "El Sauzal")
  * [Icode](/wiki/Menceyato_of_Icode "Menceyato of Icode"). _Menceyes_ : Chincanayro and [Pelicar](/wiki/Pelicar_\(mencey\) "Pelicar \(mencey\)"). Today this territory is made up of [San Juan de la Rambla](/wiki/San_Juan_de_la_Rambla "San Juan de la Rambla"), [La Guancha](/wiki/La_Guancha,_Tenerife "La Guancha, Tenerife"), [Garachico](/wiki/Garachico "Garachico") and [Icod de los Vinos](/wiki/Icod_de_los_Vinos "Icod de los Vinos").
  * [Daute](/wiki/Menceyato_of_Daute "Menceyato of Daute"). _Menceyes_ : Cocanaymo and [Romen](/wiki/Romen_\(mencey\) "Romen \(mencey\)"). Today this territory is occupied by [El Tanque](/wiki/El_Tanque "El Tanque"), [Los Silos](/wiki/Los_Silos "Los Silos"), [Buenavista del Norte](/wiki/Buenavista_del_Norte "Buenavista del Norte") and [Santiago del Teide](/wiki/Santiago_del_Teide "Santiago del Teide").
  * [Adeje](/wiki/Menceyato_of_Adeje "Menceyato of Adeje"). _Menceyes_. Atbitocazpe, [Pelinor](/wiki/Pelinor "Pelinor") and [Ichasagua](/wiki/Ichasagua "Ichasagua"). It included what today are the municipalities of [Guía de Isora](/wiki/Gu%C3%ADa_de_Isora "Guía de Isora"), [Adeje](/wiki/Adeje "Adeje") and [Vilaflor](/wiki/Vilaflor "Vilaflor")



The _achimenceyato_ of [Punta del Hidalgo](/w/index.php?title=Achimenceyato_de_Punta_del_Hidalgo&action=edit&redlink=1 "Achimenceyato de Punta del Hidalgo \(page does not exist\)") was governed by Aguahuco, a "poor noble" who was an illegitimate son of Tinerfe and Zebenzui. 

### Castilian conquest

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=5 "Edit section: Castilian conquest")]

[](/wiki/File:AlonsoFernandezdeLugo2.JPG)[Alonso Fernandez de Lugo](/wiki/Alonso_Fernandez_de_Lugo "Alonso Fernandez de Lugo") presenting the native kings of Tenerife to Ferdinand and Isabella

Tenerife was the last island of the Canaries to be conquered and the one that took the longest time to submit to the Castilian troops. Although the traditional dates of the conquest of Tenerife are established between 1494 (landing of Alonso Fernández de Lugo) and 1496 (the complete conquest of the island), attempts to annex the island of Tenerife to the [Crown of Castile](/wiki/Crown_of_Castile "Crown of Castile") date back at least to 1464.[28]

In 1464, [Diego Garcia de Herrera](/wiki/Diego_Garcia_de_Herrera "Diego Garcia de Herrera"), Lord of the Canary Islands, took symbolic possession of the island in the _Barranco del Bufadero_ (Ravine of the Bufadero),[29] signing a peace treaty with the Guanche chiefs (_menceyes_) which allowed the _mencey_ Anaga to build a fortified tower on Guanche land, where the Guanches and the Spanish held periodic treaty talks until the Guanches demolished it around 1472.[30]

In 1492 the governor of Gran Canaria Francisco Maldonado organized a raid that ended in disaster for the Spaniards when they were defeated by Anaga's warriors. In December 1493, the Catholic monarchs, Queen Isabella I of Castile and King Ferdinand II of Aragon, granted [Alonso Fernández de Lugo](/wiki/Alonso_Fern%C3%A1ndez_de_Lugo "Alonso Fernández de Lugo") the right to conquer Tenerife. Coming from [Gran Canaria](/wiki/Gran_Canaria "Gran Canaria") in April 1494, the conqueror landed on the coast of present-day Santa Cruz de Tenerife in May, and disembarked with about 2,000 men on foot and 200 on horseback.[31] After taking the fort, the army prepared to move inland, later capturing the native kings of Tenerife and presenting them to [Isabella and Ferdinand](/wiki/Catholic_Monarchs "Catholic Monarchs"). 

The _menceyes_ of Tenerife had differing responses to the conquest. They divided into the _side of peace_ (Spanish: _bando de paz_) and the _side of war_ (Spanish: _bando de guerra_). The first included the _menceyatos_ of Anaga, Güímar, Abona and Adeje. The second group consisted of the people of Tegueste, Tacoronte, Taoro, Icoden and Daute. Those opposed to the conquest fought the invaders tenaciously, resisting their rule for two years. Castillian forces under the _[Adelantado](/wiki/Adelantado "Adelantado")_ ("military governor") de Lugo suffered a crushing defeat at the hands of the Guanches in the [First Battle of Acentejo](/wiki/First_Battle_of_Acentejo "First Battle of Acentejo") on 31 May 1494, but defeated them at the [Second Battle of Acentejo](/wiki/Second_Battle_of_Acentejo "Second Battle of Acentejo") on 25 December 1494. The Guanches were eventually overcome by superior technology and the arms of the invaders, and surrendered to the Crown of Castile in 1496.[32]

### Spanish rule

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=6 "Edit section: Spanish rule")]

[](/wiki/File:Alfred_Diston_-_Square_in_the_Village_of_Los_Silos,_Tenerife_-_B1986.29.83_-_Yale_Center_for_British_Art.jpg)Square in the village of [Los Silos](/wiki/Los_Silos "Los Silos"), Tenerife, by [Alfred Diston](/wiki/Alfred_Diston "Alfred Diston"), 1827

Many of the natives died from new infectious diseases, such as [influenza](/wiki/Influenza "Influenza") and probably [smallpox](/wiki/Smallpox "Smallpox"), to which they lacked resistance or acquired [immunity](/wiki/Immunity_\(medical\) "Immunity \(medical\)"). The new colonists intermarried with the local native population. For a century after the conquest, many new colonists settled on the island, including immigrants from the diverse territories of the growing [Spanish Empire](/wiki/Iberian_Union "Iberian Union"), such as [Flanders](/wiki/Flanders "Flanders"), Italy, and Germany. 

As the population grew, it cleared Tenerife's pine forests for fuel and to make fields for agriculture for crops both for local consumption and for export. [Sugar cane](/wiki/Sugar_cane "Sugar cane") was introduced in the 1520s as a commodity crop on major plantations; it was a labor-intensive crop in all phases of cultivation and processing. In the following centuries, planters cultivated wine grapes, [cochineal](/wiki/Cochineal "Cochineal") for making dyes, and [plantains](/wiki/Plantain_\(cooking\) "Plantain \(cooking\)") for use and export.[33]

### Trade with the Americas

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=7 "Edit section: Trade with the Americas")]

[](/wiki/File:Amaro_Pargo.jpg)[Amaro Pargo](/wiki/Amaro_Pargo "Amaro Pargo") (1678–1741), [corsair](/wiki/Privateer "Privateer") and merchant from Tenerife who participated in the [Spanish treasure fleet](/wiki/Spanish_treasure_fleet "Spanish treasure fleet") (the Spanish-American trade route)

In the commerce of the Canary Islands with the Americas of the 18th century, Tenerife was the hegemonic island, since it exceeded 50% of the number of ships and 60% of the tonnage. In the islands of [La Palma](/wiki/La_Palma "La Palma") and [Gran Canaria](/wiki/Gran_Canaria "Gran Canaria"), the percentage was around 19% for the first and 7% for the second.[34] The volume of traffic between the Indies and the Canary Islands was unknown, but was very important and concentrated almost exclusively in Tenerife.[34]

Among the products that are exported were [cochineal](/wiki/Cochineal "Cochineal"), [rum](/wiki/Rum "Rum") and [sugar cane](/wiki/Sugar_cane "Sugar cane"), which were landed mainly in the ports of the Americas such as [La Guaira](/wiki/La_Guaira "La Guaira"), [Havana](/wiki/Havana "Havana"), [Campeche](/wiki/Campeche "Campeche") and [Veracruz](/wiki/Veracruz "Veracruz"). Many sailors from Tenerife joined this transcontinental maritime trade, among which the corsair [Amaro Rodríguez Felipe](/wiki/Amaro_Rodr%C3%ADguez_Felipe "Amaro Rodríguez Felipe"), more commonly known as _Amaro Pargo_ , Juan Pedro Dujardín and Bernardo de Espinosa, both companions of Amaro Pargo, among others.[35]

### Emigration to the Americas

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=8 "Edit section: Emigration to the Americas")]

Tenerife, like the other islands, has maintained a close relationship with Latin America, as both were part of the Spanish Empire. From the start of the colonization of the [New World](/wiki/New_World "New World"), many Spanish expeditions stopped at the island for supplies on their way to the Americas. They also recruited many _tinerfeños_ for their crews, who formed an integral part of the conquest expeditions. Others joined ships in search of better prospects. It is also important to note the exchange in plant and animal species that made those voyages.[36]

[](/wiki/File:Alfred_Diston_-_Woman_of_Victoria,_Tenerife_-_B1981.25.2824_-_Yale_Center_for_British_Art.jpg)Woman of La Victoria, Tenerife, by Alfred Diston, 1828

After a century and a half of relative growth, based on the [grape growing](/wiki/Vitis "Vitis") sector, numerous families emigrated, especially to [Venezuela](/wiki/Venezuela "Venezuela") and [Cuba](/wiki/Cuba "Cuba"). The [Crown](/wiki/Crown_of_Castile "Crown of Castile") wanted to encourage population of underdeveloped zones in the Americas to pre-empt the occupation by foreign forces, as had happened with the English in [Jamaica](/wiki/Jamaica "Jamaica") and the French in the [Guianas](/wiki/French_Guiana "French Guiana") and western [Hispaniola](/wiki/Hispaniola "Hispaniola") (which the French renamed as Saint-Domingue). Canary Islanders, including many _tinerfeños,_ left for the New World. 

The success in cultivation of new crops of the Americas, such as [cocoa](/wiki/Cocoa_bean "Cocoa bean") in Venezuela and tobacco in Cuba, contributed to the population exodus from towns such as Buenavista del Norte, Vilaflor, or El Sauzal in the late 17th century. The village of [San Carlos de Tenerife](/wiki/San_Carlos_de_Tenerife "San Carlos de Tenerife") was founded in 1684 by Canary Islanders on [Santo Domingo](/wiki/Santo_Domingo "Santo Domingo"). The people from Tenerife were recruited for settlement to build up the town from encroachment by French colonists established in the western side of Hispaniola. Between 1720 and 1730, the Crown moved 176 families, including many _tinerfeños,_ to the Caribbean island of [Puerto Rico](/wiki/Puerto_Rico "Puerto Rico"). In 1726, about 25 island families migrated to the Americas to collaborate on the foundation of [Montevideo](/wiki/Montevideo "Montevideo"). Four years later, in 1730, another group left that founded [San Antonio](/wiki/San_Antonio "San Antonio") the following year in what became [Texas](/wiki/Texas "Texas"). Between 1777 and 1783, more islanders emigrated from Santa Cruz de Tenerife to settle in what became [St. Bernard Parish, Louisiana](/wiki/St._Bernard_Parish,_Louisiana "St. Bernard Parish, Louisiana"), during the period when Spain ruled this former French territory west of the Mississippi River. Some groups went to Western or Spanish Florida.[36]

[](/wiki/File:Alfred_Diston_-_Man_and_woman_of_Chasna-Tenerife_-_B1975.4.282_-_Yale_Center_for_British_Art.jpg)Man and woman of Chasna, Tenerife, by Alfred Diston, 1828

Tenerife saw the arrival of the [First Fleet](/wiki/First_Fleet "First Fleet") to [Botany Bay](/wiki/Botany_Bay "Botany Bay") in June 1787, which consisted of 11 ships that departed from [Portsmouth](/wiki/Portsmouth "Portsmouth"), [England](/wiki/England "England"), on 13 May 1787 to found the [penal colony](/wiki/Penal_colony "Penal colony") that became the first European settlement in [Australia](/wiki/Australia "Australia"). The Fleet consisted of two Royal Navy vessels, three store ships and six [convict ships](/wiki/Convict_ship "Convict ship") carrying between 1,000 and 1,500 convicts, marines, seamen, civil officers and free people (accounts differ on the numbers), and a vast quantity of stores. On 3 June 1787, the fleet anchored at Santa Cruz at Tenerife. Here, fresh water, vegetables and meat were brought on board. Commander of the fleet, Capt. [Arthur Phillip](/wiki/Arthur_Phillip "Arthur Phillip") and the chief officers were entertained by the local governor, while one convict tried unsuccessfully to escape. On 10 June they set sail to cross the Atlantic to Rio de Janeiro, taking advantage of favourable trade winds and ocean currents.[37]

In June 1799, the Prussian-born naturalist [Alexander von Humboldt](/wiki/Alexander_von_Humboldt "Alexander von Humboldt") spent five full days on Tenerife on the first leg of his soon world-famous American journey (1799–1804) and climbed the Pico del Teide.[38]

Emigration to the Americas (mainly Cuba and Venezuela) continued during the 19th and early 20th century, due to the lack of economic opportunity and the relative isolation of the Canary Islands. Since the late 20th century, island protectionist economic laws and a strong development in the tourism industry have strengthened the economy and attracted new migrants. Tenerife has received numerous new residents, including the "return" of many descendants of some islanders who had departed five centuries before.[36]

### Military history

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=9 "Edit section: Military history")]

[](/wiki/File:Sir_Horatio_Nelson_when_wounded_at_Teneriffe.jpg)Admiral Nelson wounded at Tenerife

The most notable conflict was the [British invasion of Tenerife](/wiki/Battle_of_Santa_Cruz_de_Tenerife_\(1797\) "Battle of Santa Cruz de Tenerife \(1797\)") in 1797.[39] On 25 July 1797, Admiral [Horatio Nelson](/wiki/Horatio_Nelson "Horatio Nelson") launched an attack at Santa Cruz de Tenerife, now the capital of the island. After a ferocious fight which resulted in many casualties, General [Antonio Gutiérrez de Otero y Santayana](/wiki/Antonio_Guti%C3%A9rrez_de_Otero_y_Santayana "Antonio Gutiérrez de Otero y Santayana") organized a defense to repel the invaders. Whilst leading a landing party, Nelson was seriously wounded in his right arm by grapeshot or a musket ball, necessitating amputation of most of the arm.[40] Legend tells that he was wounded by the Spanish cannon _Tiger_ (Spanish: _Tigre_) as he was trying to disembark on the Paso Alto coast.[33]

On 5 September 1797, the British attempted another attack in the Puerto Santiago region, which was repelled by the inhabitants of [Santiago del Teide](/wiki/Santiago_del_Teide "Santiago del Teide"). Some threw rocks at the British from the heights of the cliffs of _[Los Gigantes](/wiki/Los_Gigantes "Los Gigantes")._

The island was also attacked by British commanders [Robert Blake](/wiki/Robert_Blake_\(admiral\) "Robert Blake \(admiral\)"), [Walter Raleigh](/wiki/Walter_Raleigh "Walter Raleigh"), [John Hawkins](/wiki/John_Hawkins_\(naval_commander\) "John Hawkins \(naval commander\)") and [Woodes Rogers](/wiki/Woodes_Rogers "Woodes Rogers").[41]

[Santa Cruz de Tenerife](/wiki/Santa_Cruz_de_Tenerife "Santa Cruz de Tenerife") was, especially during the first half of the 18th century, the most important [corsair](/wiki/Privateer "Privateer") center in the [Macaronesian](/wiki/Macaronesian "Macaronesian") region.[42][43] The recent discovery of abundant documentary evidence of significant corsair activity, carried out primarily by natives, residents, and those living on the island, confirms this fact. At the same time, the numerous pirate ships that frequented the Canary Islands waters used landing places along its coast. Such was the case of Valle de Salazar or [San Andrés](/wiki/San_Andr%C3%A9s,_Santa_Cruz_de_Tenerife "San Andrés, Santa Cruz de Tenerife"), which had acquired a reputation as a "[pirate](/wiki/Pirate "Pirate") port" until the construction of its castle or defensive tower.[44]

### Modern history

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=10 "Edit section: Modern history")]

From 1833 to 1927, Santa Cruz de Tenerife was the sole capital of the Canary Islands. In 1927, the government ordered that the capital be shared with [Las Palmas](/wiki/Las_Palmas "Las Palmas"), as it remains at present.[11][12] This change in status has encouraged development in Las Palmas. 

Tourists began visiting Tenerife from Spain, the United Kingdom, and northern Europe in large numbers in the 1890s. They especially were attracted to the destinations of the northern towns of [Puerto de la Cruz](/wiki/Puerto_de_la_Cruz,_Santa_Cruz_de_Tenerife "Puerto de la Cruz, Santa Cruz de Tenerife") and Santa Cruz de Tenerife.[45] Independent shipping business, such as the Yeoward Brothers Shipping Line, helped boost the tourist industry during this time, adding to ships that carried passengers. The naturalist [Alexander von Humboldt](/wiki/Alexander_von_Humboldt "Alexander von Humboldt") ascended the peak of Mount Teide and remarked on the beauty of the island. 

Before his rise to power, [Francisco Franco](/wiki/Francisco_Franco "Francisco Franco") was posted to Tenerife in March 1936 by a Republican government wary of his influence and political leanings. However, Franco received information and in Gran Canaria agreed to collaborate in the military coup that would result in the [Spanish Civil War](/wiki/Spanish_Civil_War "Spanish Civil War"); the Canaries fell to the Nationalists in July 1936. In the 1950s, the misery of the post-war years caused thousands of the island's inhabitants to emigrate to [Cuba](/wiki/Cuba "Cuba") and other parts of Latin America. 

Tenerife was the site of the deadliest accident ever in commercial aviation. The [Tenerife airport disaster](/wiki/Tenerife_airport_disaster "Tenerife airport disaster") occurred on 27 March 1977 when two [Boeing 747s](/wiki/Boeing_747 "Boeing 747"), [KLM](/wiki/KLM "KLM") Flight 4805 and [Pan Am](/wiki/Pan_Am "Pan Am") Flight 1736 collided on the runway at [Los Rodeos Airport](/wiki/Tenerife_North_Airport "Tenerife North Airport") in heavy fog conditions, causing the deaths of 583 passengers and crew. A few years later, [Dan Air Flight 1008](/wiki/Dan_Air_Flight_1008 "Dan Air Flight 1008") crashed into a mountain while on approach to Tenerife North, killing 146 people. The plane was travelling too close to an Iberia Air turboprop plane and was asked to go into a holding pattern.[46]

At the beginning of the 21st century, the so-called _[Tenerife flood of 2002](/wiki/Tenerife_flood_of_2002 "Tenerife flood of 2002")_ took place on 31 March of that year. It was a phenomenon of [cold drop](/wiki/Cold_drop "Cold drop") characterized by the repeated fall of torrential rains accompanied by thunder and lightning, affecting the [Santa Cruz de Tenerife metropolitan area](/wiki/Santa_Cruz_de_Tenerife_metropolitan_area "Santa Cruz de Tenerife metropolitan area") and extending in the NE direction towards the [San Andrés](/wiki/San_Andr%C3%A9s,_Santa_Cruz_de_Tenerife "San Andrés, Santa Cruz de Tenerife") area.[47] The rains caused 8 dead, 12 missing and dozens of injured.[48] In addition to the human losses, the flood caused considerable material damage, 70,000 people without light as well as the total or partial destruction of at least 400 homes. The losses were calculated at 90 million euros.[49]

In November 2005, Tenerife was the Canary Island most affected by [Tropical Storm Delta](/wiki/Tropical_Storm_Delta_\(2005\) "Tropical Storm Delta \(2005\)"). Winds of 140 km/h were recorded on the coast and almost 250 km/h on the [Teide](/wiki/Teide "Teide"), Tenerife's summit. 

## Geography

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=11 "Edit section: Geography")]

[](/wiki/File:Palm_Tree_Canyon.JPG)Palm tree canyon in inland Tenerife [](/wiki/File:Orotavatal.jpg)Panorama of the La Orotava Valley with Teide in the background [](/wiki/File:Teide2007.jpg)[Teide](/wiki/Teide "Teide") and [Roque Cinchado](/wiki/Roque_Cinchado "Roque Cinchado")

The oldest mountain ranges in Tenerife rose from the Atlantic Ocean by volcanic eruption which gave birth to the island around twelve million years ago.[50] The island as it is today was formed three million years ago by the fusion of three islands made up of the mountain ranges of [Anaga](/wiki/Anaga "Anaga"), [Teno](/wiki/Macizo_de_Teno "Macizo de Teno") and [Valle de San Lorenzo](/w/index.php?title=Valle_de_San_Lorenzo&action=edit&redlink=1 "Valle de San Lorenzo \(page does not exist\)"),[50] due to volcanic activity from [Teide](/wiki/Teide "Teide"). The volcano is visible from most parts of the island today, and the crater is 17 kilometres (11 miles) long at some points. Tenerife is the largest island of the Canary Islands and the [Macaronesia](/wiki/Macaronesia "Macaronesia") region.[7]

### Climate

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=12 "Edit section: Climate")]

Tenerife is characterized by a generally dry, warm climate. The island has two main different climatic areas, as by [Köppen climate classification](/wiki/K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification "Köppen climate classification")).[51]

The main climates are the hot semi-arid/arid climate (Köppen: _BSh_ and _BWh_) and the [subtropical](/wiki/Subtropical_climate "Subtropical climate") [Mediterranean Climate](/wiki/Mediterranean_Climate "Mediterranean Climate") (Köppen: _Csb_ and _Csa_) inland or at higher altitudes. The low altitude/coastal areas of the island have average temperatures of 18–20 °C (64–68 °F) in the winter months and 24–26 °C (75–79 °F) in the summer months. There is a high annual total of days of sunshine and low precipitation in the coastal areas. The inland/high altitude areas, such as [La Laguna](/wiki/La_Laguna "La Laguna"), are wetter, cloudier and cooler, with an average of 13–14 °C (55–57 °F) in the winter and 20–21 °C (68–70 °F) in the summer. The moderate climate of Tenerife is controlled to a great extent by the [trade winds](/wiki/Trade_winds "Trade winds"), whose [humidity](/wiki/Humidity "Humidity") is condensed principally over the north and northeast of the island, creating cloud banks that range between 600 and 1,800 metres (2,000 and 5,900 feet) in height. The cold sea currents of the Canary Islands also have a cooling effect on the coasts and its beaches, while the topography of the landscape plays a role in climatic differences on the island with its many valleys. The moderating effect of the marine air makes extreme heat a rare occurrence and frost an impossibility at sea level. The lowest recorded temperature in central Santa Cruz is 8.1 °C (46.6 °F), the coldest month on record still had a relatively mild average temperature of 15.8 °C (60.4 °F).[52] Summer temperatures are highest in August, with an average high of 29 °C (84 °F) in Santa Cruz, similar to those of places as far north as [Barcelona](/wiki/Barcelona "Barcelona") and [Mallorca](/wiki/Mallorca "Mallorca"), because of the greater maritime influence. At a higher elevation in [San Cristóbal de La Laguna](/wiki/San_Crist%C3%B3bal_de_La_Laguna "San Cristóbal de La Laguna"), the climate transitions to a [Mediterranean climate](/wiki/Mediterranean_climate "Mediterranean climate") with higher precipitation amounts and lower temperatures year round. The climate of Santa Cruz is very typical of the Canaries, albeit only slightly warmer than the [climate](/wiki/Las_Palmas#Climate "Las Palmas") of Las Palmas. 

Major climatic contrasts on the island are evident, especially during the winter months when it is possible to enjoy the warm sunshine on the coast and experience snow within 3,000 metres (10,000 feet) [above sea level](/wiki/Above_mean_sea_level "Above mean sea level") on [Teide](/wiki/Teide "Teide").[53] There are also major contrasts at low altitude, where the climate ranges from [arid](/wiki/Arid_climate "Arid climate") ([Köppen](/wiki/K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification "Köppen climate classification") _BWh_) on the southeastern side represented by Santa Cruz de Tenerife to [Mediterranean](/wiki/Mediterranean_climate "Mediterranean climate") (_Csa_ /_Csb_) on the northwestern side in Buenavista del Norte and La Orotava.[54]

The north and south of Tenerife similarly have different climatic characteristics because of the [rain shadow](/wiki/Rain_shadow "Rain shadow") effect. The windward northwestern side of the island is wetter, receiving 73 per cent of all precipitation on the island, and the relative humidity of the air is superior and the insolation inferior. The greatest rainfall measurements are registered on the windward side between 1,000 and 1,200 metres (3,300 and 3,900 feet), almost exclusively in the [La Orotava](/wiki/La_Orotava "La Orotava") mountain range.[53] Although climatic differences in rainfall and sunshine on the island exist, overall annual [precipitation](/wiki/Precipitation_\(meteorology\) "Precipitation \(meteorology\)") is low and the summer months from May to September are normally completely dry. Rainfall, similar to that of [Southern California](/wiki/Southern_California "Southern California"), can also be extremely erratic from one year to another.[55]

The centre of the island is characterized by forests because of the much higher precipitation, mostly [Canary Island pine](/wiki/Canary_Island_pine "Canary Island pine") forests in the [Teide National Park](/wiki/Teide_National_Park "Teide National Park") at altitudes from 1,300 to 2,100 metres (4,300 to 6,900 ft).[56] [Subtropical](/wiki/Subtropical "Subtropical") [cloud forests](/wiki/Cloud_forests "Cloud forests") characterised by [laurisilva](/wiki/Laurisilva "Laurisilva")[57] are commonly found in the Anaga Rural Park and Monte de Agua in the Teno Rural Park, with altitudes from 600 to 1,000 metres (2,000 to 3,300 ft) and annual averages from 15 to 19 °C (59 to 66 °F) and 600 to 1,200 metres (2,000 to 3,900 ft) in the latter.[58]

Global [climate change](/wiki/Climate_change "Climate change") has had a major impact on the island, with diminishing rainfall and hot, dry winds affecting vegetation and contributing to an increasing propensity to being subject to forest fires. On 15 August 2023, a [forest fire](/wiki/2023_Tenerife_wildfire "2023 Tenerife wildfire") determined to be caused by arson necessitated the evacuation of 12,000 residents within a week.[59]

### Water

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=13 "Edit section: Water")]

The volcanic ground of Tenerife, which is of a [porous](/wiki/Porous "Porous") and permeable character, is generally the reason why the soil is able to maximise the absorption of water on an island of low rainfall, with [condensation](/wiki/Condensation "Condensation") in forested areas and frost deposition on the summit of the island also contributory causes.[69]

Given the irregularity of precipitation and geological conditions on the island, dam construction has been avoided, so most of the water (90 percent) comes from [wells](/wiki/Water_well "Water well") and from water galleries (horizontal tunnels bored into the volcano) of which there are thousands on the island, important systems that serve to extract its hydrological resources.[70] These tunnels are very hazardous, with pockets of volcanic gas or [carbon dioxide](/wiki/Carbon_dioxide "Carbon dioxide"), causing rapid death.[71]

### Pollution and air quality

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=14 "Edit section: Pollution and air quality")]

The Canary Islands have low levels of air pollution thanks to the lack of factories and industry and the trade winds which naturally move away contaminated air from the islands. According to official data offered by the Health and Industry Ministry in Spain, Tenerife is one of the cleanest places in the country with an air pollution index below the national average.[72] Despite this, there are still agents which affect pollution levels in the island, the main [polluting agents](/wiki/Contamination "Contamination") being the [refinery at Santa Cruz](/wiki/Refiner%C3%ADa_de_Santa_Cruz_de_Tenerife "Refinería de Santa Cruz de Tenerife"), the [thermal power](/wiki/Thermal_power "Thermal power") plants at [Las Caletillas](/w/index.php?title=Las_Caletillas&action=edit&redlink=1 "Las Caletillas \(page does not exist\)") and [Granadilla](/wiki/Granadilla_de_Abona "Granadilla de Abona"), and road traffic, increased by the high level of tourism in the island. In addition on the island of Tenerife like on La Palma light pollution must be also controlled, to help the [astrophysical observatories](/wiki/Instituto_de_Astrof%C3%ADsica_de_Canarias "Instituto de Astrofísica de Canarias") located in the island's summits.[73] Water is generally of a very high quality, and all the beaches of the island of Tenerife have been catalogued by the Ministry of Health and Consumption as waters suitable for bathing.[74]

## Geology

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=15 "Edit section: Geology")]

See also: [Geology of the Canary Islands](/wiki/Geology_of_the_Canary_Islands "Geology of the Canary Islands")

[](/wiki/File:Topographic_map_of_Tenerife-en.svg)Map of Tenerife

Tenerife is a rugged volcanic island, sculpted by successive eruptions throughout its history. There are four historically recorded volcanic eruptions, none of which has led to casualties. The first occurred in 1704, when the Arafo, Fasnia and Siete Fuentes volcanoes erupted simultaneously. Two years later, in 1706, the greatest eruption occurred at Trevejo. This volcano produced great quantities of [lava](/wiki/Lava "Lava") which buried the city and port of [Garachico](/wiki/Garachico "Garachico"). The last eruption of the 18th century happened in 1798 at Cañadas de Teide, in [Chahorra](/w/index.php?title=Chahorra&action=edit&redlink=1 "Chahorra \(page does not exist\)"). The most recent eruption-in 1909-formed the Chinyero cinder cone in the municipality of [Santiago del Teide](/wiki/Santiago_del_Teide "Santiago del Teide").[75]

The island is located between 28° and 29° N and the 16° and 17° W meridian. It is situated north of the [Tropic of Cancer](/wiki/Tropic_of_Cancer "Tropic of Cancer"), occupying a central position between the other Canary Islands of [Gran Canaria](/wiki/Gran_Canaria "Gran Canaria"), [La Gomera](/wiki/La_Gomera "La Gomera") and [La Palma](/wiki/La_Palma "La Palma"). The island is about 300 km (186 mi) from the African coast, and approximately 1,000 km (621 mi) from the [Iberian Peninsula](/wiki/Iberian_Peninsula "Iberian Peninsula").[76] Tenerife is the largest island of the Canary Islands archipelago, with a surface area of 2,034.38 km2 (785 sq mi)[77] and has the longest coastline, amounting to 342 km (213 mi).[78]

In addition, the highest point, Mount Teide, with an elevation of 3,715 m (12,188 ft) [above sea level](/wiki/Above_mean_sea_level "Above mean sea level") is the highest point in all of Spain,[79] is also the third largest volcano in the world from its base in the bottom of the sea. For this reason, Tenerife is the [10th-highest island worldwide](/wiki/List_of_islands_by_highest_point "List of islands by highest point"). It comprises about 200 small barren islets or large rocks including [Roques de Anaga](/wiki/Roques_de_Anaga "Roques de Anaga"), [Roque de Garachico](/wiki/Roque_de_Garachico "Roque de Garachico"), and [Fasnia](/wiki/Fasnia "Fasnia") adding a further 213,835 m2 (2,301,701 sq ft) to the total area.[77]

### Origins and geological formation

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=16 "Edit section: Origins and geological formation")]

[](/wiki/File:Tenerife_formation.png)Tenerife formation

Tenerife is a volcanic island that has built up from the ocean floor during the last 20 million years.[80][81]

Underwater fissural eruptions produced [pillow lava](/wiki/Pillow_lava "Pillow lava"), which are produced by the rapid cooling of the magma when it comes in contact with water, obtaining their peculiar shape. This pillow-lava accumulated, constructing the base of the island underneath the sea. As this accumulation approached the surface of the water, gases erupted from the magma due to the reduction of the surrounding pressure. The volcanic eruptions became more violent and had a more explosive character, and resulted in the forming of peculiar geological fragments.[80]

After long-term accumulation of these fragments, the birth of the island occurred at the end of the [Miocene epoch](/wiki/Miocene "Miocene"). The zones on Tenerife known as [Macizo de Teno](/wiki/Macizo_de_Teno "Macizo de Teno"), [Macizo de Anaga](/wiki/Macizo_de_Anaga "Macizo de Anaga") and [Macizo de Adeje](/w/index.php?title=Macizo_de_Adeje&action=edit&redlink=1 "Macizo de Adeje \(page does not exist\)") were formed seven million years ago; these formations are called the _Ancient Basaltic Series_ or _Series I_. These zones were actually three separate islands lying in what is now the extreme west, east, and south of Tenerife.[82]

A second volcanic cycle called the _Post-Miocene Formations_ or _Latest Series II, III, IV_ began three million years ago. This was a much more intense volcanic cycle, which united the Macizo de Teno, Macizo de Anaga and Macizo de Adeje into one island. This new structure, called the _Pre-Cañadas Structure_ (_Edificio pre-Cañadas_), would be the foundation for what is called the _Cañadas Structure I_. The Cañadas Structure I experienced various collapses and emitted explosive material that produced the area known as _Bandas del sur_ (in the present-day south-southeast of Tenerife).[80]

Subsequently, upon the ruins of Cañadas Structure I emerged _Cañadas Structure II_ , which was 2,500 metres (8,200 ft) [above sea level](/wiki/Height_above_sea_level "Height above sea level") and emerged with intense explosive activity. About one million years ago, the Dorsal Range (_Cordillera Dorsal_) emerged by means of fissural volcanic activity occurring amidst the remains of the older Ancient Basaltic Series (Series I). This Dorsal Range emerged as the highest and the longest volcanic structure in the Canary Islands; it was 1,600 metres (5,200 ft) high and 25 kilometres (16 mi) long.[80]

About 800,000 years ago, two gravitational [landslides](/wiki/Volcanic_landslide "Volcanic landslide") occurred, giving rise to the present-day valleys of La Orotava and Güímar.[80] Finally, around 200,000 years ago, the giant Icod landslide occurred followed by eruptions that raised the _Pico Viejo-Teide_[83] in the centre of the island, over the _Las Cañadas caldera_.[80]

### Orography and landscape

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=17 "Edit section: Orography and landscape")]

The uneven and steep [orography](/wiki/Orography "Orography") of the island and its variety of climates has resulted in a diversity of landscapes and geographical and geological formations, from the Teide National Park with its extensive [pine](/wiki/Pine "Pine") forests, juxtaposed against the volcanic landscape at the summit of Teide and [Malpaís de Güímar](/wiki/Malpa%C3%ADs_de_G%C3%BC%C3%ADmar "Malpaís de Güímar"), to the Acantilados de Los Gigantes (Cliffs of the Giants) with its vertical precipices. Semidesert areas exist in the south with drought-resistant plants. Other areas range from those protected and enclosed in mountains such as Montaña Roja and Montaña Pelada, the valleys and forests with subtropical vegetation and climate, to those with deep gorges and precipices such as at Anaga and Teno. 

#### Central heights

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=18 "Edit section: Central heights")]

The principal structures in Tenerife, make the central highlands, with the Teide–[Pico Viejo](/wiki/Pico_Viejo "Pico Viejo") complex and the Las Cañadas areas as most prominent. It comprises a semi-[caldera](/wiki/Caldera "Caldera") of about 130 km2 (50 sq mi) in area, originated by several geological processes explained under the _Origin and formation_ section. The area is partially occupied by the Teide-Pico Viejo strato-volcano and completed by the materials emitted in the different eruptions that took place. A known formation called _Los Azulejos_ , composed by green-tinted rocks were created by hydrothermal processes.[33][80][53]

South of La Caldera is [Guajara Mountain](/wiki/Mount_Guajara "Mount Guajara"), which has an elevation of 2,718 metres (8,917 feet), rising above Teide National Park. At the bottom, is an [endorheic basin](/wiki/Endorheic_basin "Endorheic basin") flanked with very fine sedimentary material which has been deposited from its volcanic processes, and is known as Llano de Ucanca.[33][80][53]

The peak of [Teide](/wiki/Teide "Teide"), at 3,715 metres (12,188 feet) [above sea level](/wiki/Above_mean_sea_level "Above mean sea level") and more than 7,500 metres (24,606 feet) above the ocean floor, is the highest point of the island, Spanish territory and in the Atlantic Ocean. The volcano is the third largest on the planet, and its central location,[_[clarification needed](/wiki/Wikipedia:Please_clarify "Wikipedia:Please clarify")_] substantial size, looming silhouette in the distance and its snowy landscape in winter give it a unique nature.[84] The original settlers considered Teide a god and Teide was a place of worship. 

In 1954, the whole area around it was declared a [national park](/wiki/National_park "National park"), with further expansion later on. In addition, in June 2007 it was recognised by [UNESCO](/wiki/UNESCO "UNESCO") as a [World Heritage site](/wiki/World_Heritage_sites "World Heritage sites").[85] To the west lies the volcano [Pico Viejo](/wiki/Pico_Viejo "Pico Viejo") (Old Peak). On one side of it, is the volcano Chahorra o Narices del Teide, where the last eruption occurred in the vicinity of Mount Teide in 1798. 

The Teide is one of the 16 [Decade Volcanoes](/wiki/Decade_Volcanoes "Decade Volcanoes") identified by the International Association of Volcanology and Chemistry of the Earth's Interior (IAVCEI) as being worthy of particular study in light of their history of large, destructive eruptions and proximity to populated areas. 

**Tallest mountains on Tenerife:**

Peak  | Elevation (meters)  | Elevation (feet)   
---|---|---  
[Mount Teide](/wiki/Teide "Teide") | 3,715  | 12,198   
[Pico Viejo](/wiki/Pico_Viejo "Pico Viejo") | 3,135  | 10,285   
[Montaña Blanca](/wiki/Monta%C3%B1a_Blanca "Montaña Blanca") | 2,748  | 9,016   
[Guajara](/wiki/Mount_Guajara "Mount Guajara") | 2,718  | 8,917   
  
#### Massifs

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=19 "Edit section: Massifs")]

[](/wiki/File:Anaga_2006.jpg)The uneven contours of the Anaga massif

The Anaga massif (_[Macizo de Anaga](/wiki/Macizo_de_Anaga "Macizo de Anaga")_), at the northeastern end of the island, has an irregular and rugged topographical profile where, despite its generally modest elevations, the Cruz de Taborno reaches a height of 1,024 metres (3,360 feet). Due to the age of its material (5.7 million years), its deep erosive processes, and the dense network of [dikes](/wiki/Dike_\(geology\) "Dike \(geology\)") piercing the massif, its surface exposes numerous outcroppings of both [phonolitic](/wiki/Phonolite "Phonolite") and [trachytic](/wiki/Trachyte "Trachyte") origin. A large number of steep-walled gorges are present, penetrating deeply into the terrain. Vertical cuts dominate the Anagan coast, with infrequent beaches of rocks or black sand between them; the few that exist generally coincide with the mouths of gorges.[33][80][53]

[](/wiki/File:Punta_teno.jpg)Teno massif—Cliffs of the Giants area

The Teno massif (_[Macizo de Teno](/wiki/Macizo_de_Teno "Macizo de Teno")_) is located on the northwestern edge of the island. Like Anaga, it includes an area of outcroppings and deep gorges formed by erosion. However, the materials here are older (about 7.4 million years old). Mount Gala represents its highest elevation at 1,342 metres (4,403 feet). The most unusual landscape of this massif is found on its southern coast, where the [Acantilados de Los Gigantes](/wiki/Acantilados_de_Los_Gigantes "Acantilados de Los Gigantes") ("Cliffs of the Giants") present vertical walls reaching heights of 500 metres (1,600 feet) in some places.[33][80][53]

The Adeje massif (_Macizo de Adeje_) is situated on the southern tip of the island. Its main landmark is the Roque del Conde ("Count's Rock"), with an elevation of 1,001 metres (3,284 feet). This massif is not as impressive as the others due to its diminished initial structure, since in addition to with the site's greater geologic age it has experienced severe erosion of its material, thereby losing its original appearance and extent.[33][80][53]

#### Dorsals

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=20 "Edit section: Dorsals")]

The Dorsal mountain [ridge](/wiki/Ridge "Ridge") or _Dorsal of Pedro Gil_ covers the area from the start at Mount La Esperanza, at a height of about 750 m (2,461 ft), to the center of the island, near the _Caldera de Las Cañadas_ , with Izaña, as its highest point at 2,390 m (7,841 ft) ([MSLP](/wiki/Atmospheric_pressure "Atmospheric pressure")). These mountains have been created due to basaltic fissural volcanism through one of the axis that gave birth to the vulcanism of this area.[33][80][53]

The Abeque Dorsal was formed by a chain of volcanoes that join the Teno with the central insular peak of Teide-Pico Viejo starting from another of the three axis of Tenerife's geological structures. On this dorsal we find the historic volcano of Chinyero whose last eruption happened in 1909.[33][80][53]

The South Dorsal or _Dorsal of Adeje_ is part of the last of the structural axis. The remains of this massive rock show the primordial land, also showing the alignment of small volcanic cones and rocks around this are in Tenerife's South.[33][80][53]

#### Valleys and ravines

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=21 "Edit section: Valleys and ravines")]

[Valleys](/wiki/Valley "Valley") are another of the island's features. The most important are [Valle de La Orotava](/wiki/Valle_de_La_Orotava "Valle de La Orotava") and [Valle de Güímar](/wiki/Valle_de_G%C3%BC%C3%ADmar "Valle de Güímar"), both formed by the mass sliding of great quantities of material towards the sea, creating a depression of the land. Other valleys tend to be between hills formed by deposits of sediments from nearby slopes, or simply wide ravines which in their evolution have become typical valleys.[33][80][53]

Tenerife has a large number of ravines, which are a characteristic element of the landscape, caused by erosion from [surface runoff](/wiki/Surface_runoff "Surface runoff") over a long period. Notable ravines include Ruiz, Fasnia and Güímar, [Infierno](/wiki/Barranco_del_Infierno_\(Tenerife\) "Barranco del Infierno \(Tenerife\)"), and Erques, all of which have been designated protected natural areas by Canarian institutions.[33][80][53]

[](/wiki/File:Panom-puerto.jpg)Panorama of [Valle de La Orotava](/wiki/Valle_de_La_Orotava "Valle de La Orotava")

#### Coastline

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=22 "Edit section: Coastline")]

The coasts of Tenerife are typically rugged and steep, particularly on the north of the island. However, the island has 67.14 kilometres (41.72 miles) of beaches, such as the one at [El Médano](/wiki/El_M%C3%A9dano "El Médano"), surpassed only in this respect by the island of [Fuerteventura](/wiki/Fuerteventura "Fuerteventura").[86] There are many black sand pebble beaches on the northern coast, while on the south and south-west coast of the island, the beaches have typically much finer and clearer sand with lighter tones.[33][80][53]

#### Volcanic tubes

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=23 "Edit section: Volcanic tubes")]

[Lava tubes](/wiki/Lava_tubes "Lava tubes") are volcanic caves usually in the form of tunnels formed within lava flows more or less fluid reogenética duration of the activity. Among the many existing volcanic tubes on the island stands out the [Cueva del Viento](/wiki/Cueva_del_Viento "Cueva del Viento"), located in the northern town of [Icod de los Vinos](/wiki/Icod_de_los_Vinos "Icod de los Vinos"), which is the largest volcanic tunnel in the [European Union](/wiki/European_Union "European Union") and one of the largest in the world, for a long time considered the largest in the world. 

## Flora and fauna

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=24 "Edit section: Flora and fauna")]

[](/wiki/File:Chamorga,_Macizo_de_Anaga,_Tenerife,_Spain_35.jpg)Chamorga, in the [Macizo de Anaga](/wiki/Macizo_de_Anaga "Macizo de Anaga") [](/wiki/File:The_butterflies_and_moths_of_Teneriffe_\(1894\)_\(20484502286\).jpg)_Lepidoptera_ illustrations in _[The butterflies and moths of Teneriffe](/wiki/The_butterflies_and_moths_of_Teneriffe "The butterflies and moths of Teneriffe")_ (1894)

The island of Tenerife has a remarkable [ecosystem diversity](/wiki/Ecosystem_diversity "Ecosystem diversity") in spite of its small surface area, which is a consequence of the special environmental conditions on the island, where its distinct orography modifies the general climatic conditions at a local level, producing a significant variety of [microclimates](/wiki/Microclimate "Microclimate"). This diversity of microclimates allows some 1400 species of plants to exist on the island, with well over 100 of these endemic to Tenerife.[87] The fauna of Tenerife includes some 400 species of fish, 56 birds, five reptiles, two amphibians, 13 land mammals, thousands of invertebrates, and several species of sea turtles and cetaceans. 

The vegetation of Tenerife can be divided into six major zones that are directly related to altitude and the direction in which they face. 

[](/wiki/File:Luftbild_vom_Vulkan_Chinyero_auf_Teneriffa,_Spanien_\(48225246682\).jpg)Forested areas populated by _[Pinus](/wiki/Pinus "Pinus")_ in [El Chinyero Special Nature Reserve](/w/index.php?title=El_Chinyero_Special_Nature_Reserve&action=edit&redlink=1 "El Chinyero Special Nature Reserve \(page does not exist\)") [[es](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reserva_natural_especial_del_Chinyero "es:Reserva natural especial del Chinyero")] [](/wiki/File:Eidechsen_Teide.jpg)_[Gallotia galloti](/wiki/Gallotia_galloti "Gallotia galloti")_ , a wall lizard species endemic to Tenerife

  * Lower xerophytic zone: 0–700 metres (0–2,297 feet). Xerophytic shrubs that are well adapted to long dry spells, intense sunshine and strong winds. Many endemic species: spurges, cactus spurge (_[Euphorbia canariensis](/wiki/Euphorbia_canariensis "Euphorbia canariensis")_), wax plants (_[Ceropegia](/wiki/Ceropegia "Ceropegia")_ spp.), etc.
  * Thermophile forest: 200–600 metres (660–1,970 feet). Transition zone with moderate temperatures and rainfall, but the area has been deteriorated by human activity. Many endemic species: juniper (_[Juniperus cedrus](/wiki/Juniperus_cedrus "Juniperus cedrus")_), dragon trees (_[Dracaena draco](/wiki/Dracaena_draco "Dracaena draco")_), palm trees (_[Phoenix canariensis](/wiki/Phoenix_canariensis "Phoenix canariensis")_), etc.
  * [Laurel forest](/wiki/Laurel_forest "Laurel forest"): 500–1,000 metres (1,600–3,300 feet). Dense forest of large trees, descendants of [tertiary](/wiki/Tertiary_period "Tertiary period") age flora, situated in a zone of frequent rainfall and mists. A wide variety of species with abundant undergrowth of bushes, herbaceous plants, and ferns. Laurels, holly (_[Ilex canariensis](/wiki/Ilex_canariensis "Ilex canariensis")_), ebony (_[Persea indica](/wiki/Persea_indica "Persea indica")_), mahogany (_[Apollonias barbujana](/wiki/Apollonias_barbujana "Apollonias barbujana")_), etc.
  * Wax myrtle: 1,000–1,500 metres (3,300–4,900 feet). A dryer vegetation, poorer in species. It replaces the degraded laurel forest. Of great forestry importance. Wax myrtles (_[Myrica faya](/wiki/Myrica_faya "Myrica faya")_), tree heath (_[Erica arborea](/wiki/Erica_arborea "Erica arborea")_), holly, etc.
  * Pine forest: 800–2,000 metres (2,600–6,600 feet). Open pine forest, with thin and unvaried undergrowth. Canary Island pine (_[Pinus canariensis](/wiki/Pinus_canariensis "Pinus canariensis")_), broom (_[Genista canariensis](/wiki/Genista_canariensis "Genista canariensis")_), rock rose (_[Cistus](/wiki/Cistus "Cistus")_ spp.), etc.
  * High mountain: over 2,000 metres (6,600 feet). Dry climate, intense solar radiation and extreme temperatures. Flora well adapted to the conditions.[87]



### Prehistoric fauna

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=25 "Edit section: Prehistoric fauna")]

[](/wiki/File:Canariomys_bravoi_skull.JPG)Skull of _Canariomys bravoi_

Before the arrival of humans, the Canary Islands were inhabited by certain endemic animals, now mostly extinct. These animals reached [larger than usual sizes](/wiki/Megafauna "Megafauna"), because of a phenomenon called [island gigantism](/wiki/Island_gigantism "Island gigantism"). Among these species, the best known in Tenerife were: 

  * The giant rat (_[Canariomys bravoi](/wiki/Canariomys_bravoi "Canariomys bravoi")_): Fossils mostly dating from the [Pliocene](/wiki/Pliocene "Pliocene") and [Pleistocene](/wiki/Pleistocene "Pleistocene"). Its skull reached up to 7 centimeters long, so it could have reached the size of a rabbit, which would make it quite large compared to European species of rats. Tenerife Giant Rat fossils usually occur in caves and volcanic tubes associated with _Gallotia goliath_.[88]
  * The slender-billed greenfinch (_[Chloris aurelioi](/wiki/Slender-billed_greenfinch "Slender-billed greenfinch")_), an extinct [greenfinch](/wiki/Greenfinch "Greenfinch") from the [Holocene](/wiki/Holocene "Holocene").[89]
  * The long-legged bunting (_[Emberiza alcoveri](/wiki/Long-legged_bunting "Long-legged bunting")_), a flightless bunting with long legs and short wings known from Pleistocene to [Holocene](/wiki/Holocene "Holocene") cave deposits, and one of the few flightless passerines known to science, all of which are now extinct.[90]
  * The giant lizard (_[Gallotia goliath](/wiki/Gallotia_goliath "Gallotia goliath")_) inhabited Tenerife from the Holocene until the fifteenth century AD. It was a specimen reaching a length of 120 to 125 centimeters (47.2 to 49.2 inches).[91]
  * The giant tortoise (_[Geochelone burchardi](/wiki/Geochelone_burchardi "Geochelone burchardi")_): A large tortoise, similar to those currently found in some oceanic islands like the [Galápagos Islands](/wiki/Gal%C3%A1pagos_Islands "Galápagos Islands") in the Pacific Ocean and the [Seychelles](/wiki/Seychelles "Seychelles") in the Indian Ocean. Remains found date from the [Miocene](/wiki/Miocene "Miocene"); this tortoise may have inhabited the island until the Upper [Pleistocene](/wiki/Pleistocene "Pleistocene"), apparently becoming extinct because of volcanic events long before the arrival of humans. Its shell measured approximately 65 to 94 centimetres (26 to 37 inches).[92]



### Protected natural areas

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=26 "Edit section: Protected natural areas")]

[](/wiki/File:Espacios_protegidos_Tenerife-en.png)Map showing the classification of protected areas in Tenerife

Nearly half of the island territory (48.6 percent),[93] is under protection from the [Red Canaria de Espacios Naturales Protegidos](/wiki/Red_Canaria_de_Espacios_Naturales_Protegidos "Red Canaria de Espacios Naturales Protegidos") (Canary Islands Network for Protected Natural Areas). Of the 146 protected sites under control of network in the Canary Islands archipelago,[94] a total of 43 are located in Tenerife, the most protected island in the group.[95] The network has criteria which places areas under its observation under eight different categories of protection, all of them are represented in Tenerife. Aside from [Parque Nacional del Teide](/wiki/Teide_National_Park "Teide National Park"), it counts the Parque Natural de Canarias (Crown Forest), two rural parks (Anaga and Teno), four integral natural reserves, six special natural reserves, a total of fourteen natural monuments, nine protected landscapes and up to six sites of scientific interest. Also located on the island [Macizo de Anaga](/wiki/Macizo_de_Anaga "Macizo de Anaga") since 2015 is [Biosphere Reserve](/wiki/Biosphere_Reserve "Biosphere Reserve")[17] and is the place that has the largest number of endemic species in Europe.[17]

In contrast to the land-based protected areas, Tenerife also boasts significant marine protected natural areas. Among these is the Zona de Especial Conservación Teno-Rasca (Teno-Rasca Special Area of Conservation), a marine protected area established in 2013.[96] This marine protected area off the coast of Tenerife is known for its ecological significance and biodiversity, including resident populations of cetaceans such as bottlenose dolphins and pilot whales.[97] It is also known as the Tenerife-La Gomera Marine Area and became the first European designated Whale Heritage Area in January 2021.[98]

## Administration

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=27 "Edit section: Administration")]

### Law and order

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=28 "Edit section: Law and order")]

[](/wiki/File:Santa_Cruz_Innenhof_der_Presidencia_del_gobierno_de_canarias_\(fcm\).jpg)Building of the Presidency of the Canaries Autonomous Government in Santa Cruz

Tenerife island's government resides with the [Cabildo Insular de Tenerife](/wiki/Cabildo_de_Tenerife "Cabildo de Tenerife")[99] located at the Plaza de España at the island's capital city ([Palacio Insular de Tenerife](/wiki/Palacio_Insular_de_Tenerife "Palacio Insular de Tenerife")). The political Canary organization does not have a provincial government body but instead each island has its own government at their own [Cabildo](/wiki/Cabildo_\(council\) "Cabildo \(council\)"). Since its creation in March 1913 it has a series of capabilities and duties, stated in the Canary Autonomy Statutes (Spanish: _Estatuto de Autonomía de Canarias_) and regulated by Law 14/1990, of 26 July 1990, of the _Régimen Jurídico de las Administraciones Públicas de Canarias_.[100]

The Cabildo is composed of the following administrative offices; Presidency, Legislative Body, Government Council, Informative Commissions, Spokesman's office. 

### Government

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=29 "Edit section: Government")]

Tenerife is an autonomous territory of Spain. The island has a tiered-government system and a special status within the European Union in which it holds lower tax rates compared to other regions. Santa Cruz is the seat of half of the regional government departments and parliament and it is there that the governor is elected by the Canarian people. Afterwards, they are appointed by Madrid. There are fifteen members of parliament who work together in passing legislation, organising budgets and improving the economy.[101]

### Municipalities

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=30 "Edit section: Municipalities")]

The island, itself part of a [Spanish province](/wiki/Province_\(Spain\) "Province \(Spain\)") named [Santa Cruz de Tenerife](/wiki/Province_of_Santa_Cruz_de_Tenerife "Province of Santa Cruz de Tenerife"), is divided administratively into 31 municipalities. It is the island with the most municipalities in the Canary archipelago. 

Only three municipalities are landlocked: [Tegueste](/wiki/Tegueste "Tegueste"), [El Tanque](/wiki/El_Tanque "El Tanque") and [Vilaflor](/wiki/Vilaflor "Vilaflor"). Vilaflor is the municipality with the highest altitude in the Canaries (its capital is 1,400 metres (4,600 ft) high). 

The largest municipality with an area of 207.31 square kilometres (80.04 square miles) is La Orotava, which covers much of the Teide National Park. The smallest town on the island and of the archipelago is [Puerto de la Cruz](/wiki/Puerto_de_la_Cruz "Puerto de la Cruz"), with an area of just 8.73 square kilometres (3 square miles).[77]

It is also common to find internal division, in that some cities make up a metropolitan area within a municipality, notably the cities of Santa Cruz and La Laguna.

### Counties

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=31 "Edit section: Counties")]

The counties of Tenerife have no official recognition, but there is a consensus among geographers about them:[106]

  * Abona
  * Acentejo
  * Anaga
  * Valle de Güímar
  * Icod
  * Isora
  * Valle de La Orotava
  * Teno



### Flags and heraldry

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=32 "Edit section: Flags and heraldry")]

[](/wiki/File:Coat_of_Arms_of_Tenerife.svg)Coat-of-arms of Tenerife

The [flag of Tenerife](/wiki/Flag_of_Tenerife "Flag of Tenerife") was originally adopted in 1845 by the navy at its base in the Port of Santa Cruz de Tenerife. Later, and at present, this flag represents all the island of Tenerife. It was approved by the [Cabildo Insular de Tenerife](/wiki/Cabildo_de_Tenerife "Cabildo de Tenerife") and the Order of the Government of the Canary Islands on 9 May 1989 and published on 22 May in the government report of the Canary Islands and made official.[107]

The [coat-of-arms](/wiki/Coat-of-arms "Coat-of-arms") of Tenerife was granted by royal decree on 23 March 1510 by [Ferdinand II](/wiki/Ferdinand_II_of_Aragon "Ferdinand II of Aragon") at [Madrid](/wiki/Madrid "Madrid") in the name of [Joan I](/wiki/Joanna_of_Castile "Joanna of Castile"), Queen of Castile. The coat-of-arms has a field of gold, with an image of [Saint Michael](/wiki/Saint_Michael "Saint Michael") ([patron saint](/wiki/Patron_saint "Patron saint") of the island) above a mountain depicted in brownish, natural colors. Flames erupt from the mountain, symbolizing El Teide. Below this mountain is depicted the island itself in [vert](/wiki/Vert_\(heraldry\) "Vert \(heraldry\)") on top of blue and silver waves. To the right there is a castle in [gules](/wiki/Gules "Gules"), and to the left, a lion [rampant](/wiki/Attitude_\(heraldry\) "Attitude \(heraldry\)") in gules. The shield that the Cabildo Insular, or Island Government, uses is slightly different from that used by the city government of La Laguna, which uses a motto in the arms' border and also includes some palm branches.[108]

## Natural symbols

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=33 "Edit section: Natural symbols")]

Main article: [List of animal and plant symbols of the Canary Islands](/wiki/List_of_animal_and_plant_symbols_of_the_Canary_Islands "List of animal and plant symbols of the Canary Islands")

The official natural symbols associated with Tenerife are the bird [blue chaffinch](/wiki/Tenerife_blue_chaffinch "Tenerife blue chaffinch") (_[Fringilla teydea](/wiki/Fringilla_teydea "Fringilla teydea")_) and the [Canary Islands dragon tree](/wiki/Dracaena_draco "Dracaena draco") (_Dracaena draco_) tree.[109]

## Demographics

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=34 "Edit section: Demographics")]

Foreign nationalities (2018)[110]
