15 | [Ecuador](/wiki/Ecuador "Ecuador") | 2,073   
  
According to INE data as at 1 January 2024, Tenerife has the largest population of the seven Canary Islands and was the most populated island of Spain with 964,021 officially estimated inhabitants,[111] of whom about 22.0 percent (211,436) lived in the capital, Santa Cruz de Tenerife, and 40 percent in the [metropolitan area of Santa Cruz–La Laguna](/wiki/Santa_Cruz_de_Tenerife_metropolitan_area "Santa Cruz de Tenerife metropolitan area").[112] Santa Cruz de Tenerife and the city of [San Cristóbal de La Laguna](/wiki/San_Crist%C3%B3bal_de_La_Laguna "San Cristóbal de La Laguna") are physically one urban area, so that together (and including Tegueste and El Rosario) they have a population of 581,947 inhabitants.[113][114]

Tenerife has two other metropolitan areas recognized by the [Ministry of Development](/wiki/Ministry_of_Development_\(Spain\) "Ministry of Development \(Spain\)"); the [Tenerife South metropolitan area](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife_South_metropolitan_area&action=edit&redlink=1 "Tenerife South metropolitan area \(page does not exist\)") with 215,532 inhabitants (2024) and the [La Orotava Valley metropolitan area](/w/index.php?title=La_Orotava_Valley_metropolitan_area&action=edit&redlink=1 "La Orotava Valley metropolitan area \(page does not exist\)") with 111,606 inhabitants (2024).[115]

After the city of Santa Cruz the major towns and municipalities as at the start of 2024 are [San Cristóbal de La Laguna](/wiki/San_Crist%C3%B3bal_de_La_Laguna "San Cristóbal de La Laguna") (160,258), [Arona](/wiki/Arona,_Tenerife "Arona, Tenerife") (86,624), [Granadilla de Abona](/wiki/Granadilla_de_Abona "Granadilla de Abona") (57,143), [Adeje](/wiki/Adeje "Adeje") (50,549), [La Orotava](/wiki/La_Orotava "La Orotava") (42,585), [Los Realejos](/wiki/Los_Realejos "Los Realejos") (37,522) and [Puerto de la Cruz](/wiki/Puerto_de_la_Cruz "Puerto de la Cruz") (31,377). All other municipalities have fewer than 30,000 inhabitants, the smallest municipality being [Vilaflor](/wiki/Vilaflor "Vilaflor") with a population of 1,501. 

The island has high rates of resident population not registered in population censuses, primarily tourists. This has made several sources point out that more than one million inhabitants actually live on the island of Tenerife today.[116] The island is also the most multicultural in the archipelago, with the highest number of registered foreigners (44.9% of registered in Canary Islands), which represent 14% of the total population of the island.[117] Tenerife stands out in the context of the archipelago, by also concentrating the largest presence of non-EU foreign population.[118]

Tenerife has three large population areas that are very different and distributed: The Metropolitan Zone, the South Zone and the North Zone. With several protected natural parks — 48.6% of the territory — and an urban swarm around the island, in the last half century the insular coastal platform has become a highly urbanized metropolitan system. The high level of population in a relatively small territory — more than 900,000 inhabitants in just over 2,000 km2 (770 sq mi) — and the strong urbanization have turned the island of Tenerife, in the words of architect Federico García Barba; on an "island-city" or "island-ring".[119][120]

[](/wiki/File:Gr%C3%A1fico.png)Demographic evolution of Tenerife

Recently Tenerife has experienced population growth significantly higher than the national average. In 1990, there were 663,306 registered inhabitants, which increased to 709,365 in 2000, an increase of 46,059 or an annual growth of 0.69 percent. However, between 2000 and 2007, the population rose by 155,705 to 865,070, an annual increase of 3.14 percent.[121]

These results reflect the general trend in Spain where, since 2000, immigration has reversed the general slowdown in population growth, following the collapse in the birth rate from 1976. However, since 2001 the overall growth rate in Spain has been around 1.7 percent per year, compared with 3.14 percent on Tenerife, one of the largest increases in the country.[122]

## Economy

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=35 "Edit section: Economy")]

[](/wiki/File:Tenerife-phil5.jpg)Harbour

Tenerife is the economic capital of the Canary Islands.[123] At present, Tenerife is the island with the highest [GDP](/wiki/Gross_domestic_product "Gross domestic product") in the archipelago (~€25 billion) and has a GDP [per capita](/wiki/Per_capita_income "Per capita income") of ~€26,000.[124] Even though Tenerife's economy is highly specialized in the [service sector](/wiki/Tertiary_sector_of_the_economy "Tertiary sector of the economy"), which makes 78% of its total production capacity, the importance of the rest of the economic sectors is key to its production development. In this sense, the primary sector, which only represents 1.98% of the total product, groups activities that are important to the sustainable development of the island's economy. The energy sector which contributes 2.85% has a primary role in the development of renewable energy sources. The industrial sector which shares in 5.80% is a growing activity in the island, vis-a-vis the new possibilities created by technological advances. Finally, the construction sector with 11.29% of the total production has a strategic priority, because it is a sector with relative stability which permits multiple possibilities of development and employment opportunities.[125]

### Tourism

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=36 "Edit section: Tourism")]

[](/wiki/File:Puertodelacruztf.JPG)[Puerto de la Cruz](/wiki/Puerto_de_la_Cruz "Puerto de la Cruz"), in the North, during winter, featuring background snowy mountains

Tourism is the most prominent industry in the Canaries, which are one of the major tourist destinations in the world. Tenerife is the most visited island in the archipelago[8] and one of the most important tourist destinations in Spain.[9]

In 2014, 11,473,600 foreign tourists came to the Canary Islands. Tenerife had 4,171,384 arrivals that year, excluding the numbers for Spanish tourists which make up an additional 30% of total arrivals. According to last year's Canarian Statistics Centre's (ISTAC) Report on Tourism the greatest number of tourists from any one country come from the United Kingdom, with more than 3,980,000 tourists in 2014. In second place comes Germany followed by Sweden, Norway, the Netherlands, France, Ireland, Belgium, Italy, Denmark, Finland, Switzerland, Poland, Russia and Austria.[_[citation needed](/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_]

Tourism is more prevalent in the south of the island, which is hotter and drier and has many large resorts such as [Playa de las Americas](/wiki/Playa_de_las_Americas "Playa de las Americas") and [Los Cristianos](/wiki/Los_Cristianos "Los Cristianos"). More recently coastal development has spread northwards from Playa de las Americas and now encompasses the former small enclave of La Caleta. According to the _Moratoria_ act passed by the Canarian Parliament in Santa Cruz de Tenerife, no more hotels will be built on the island unless they are classified as 5 star-quality and comprise different services such as golf courses or convention facilities. This act was passed with the goal of improving the standard of tourism service and promoting environmentally conscious development. 

[](/wiki/File:Playa_de_las_americas_Tenerife.jpg)Sunset at the beach Playa De Las Americas, Tenerife

The area known as [Costa Adeje](/wiki/Costa_Adeje "Costa Adeje") has many facilities and leisure opportunities such as shopping centres, golf courses, restaurants, water parks (the most well-known being [Siam Park](/wiki/Siam_Park_\(Tenerife\) "Siam Park \(Tenerife\)")), animal parks, and a theatre suitable for musicals or a convention centre.[126] There are many boats offering whale watching tours from the harbour of Puerto Colon. The deep waters off the coast of Costa Adeje are home to several pods of [pilot whales](/wiki/Pilot_whale "Pilot whale").[127]

In the more lush and green north of the island, the main focus of development for tourism has been in the town of [Puerto de la Cruz](/wiki/Puerto_de_la_Cruz "Puerto de la Cruz"). Puerto de la Cruz is home to the [SeaWorld](/wiki/SeaWorld "SeaWorld")-owned zoo, [Loro Parque](/wiki/Loro_Parque "Loro Parque"),[128] which has been accused of mistreatment of animals in its captivity, including [orcas](/wiki/Orca "Orca")[129] and is currently boycotted by major travel agents including [Thomas Cook](/wiki/Thomas_Cook_Group "Thomas Cook Group").[130]

In the 19th century large numbers of foreign tourists came, especially British, showing interest in the agriculture of the islands. With the world wars, the tourism sector weakened, but the second half of the 20th century brought renewed interest. Initial emphasis was on Puerto de la Cruz, and for all the attractions that the Valle de la Orotava offered. By 1980, tourism was focused in south Tenerife. The emphasis was on cities like Arona or Adeje, shifting to tourist centres like Los Cristianos or Playa de Las Americas, which now house 65% of the hotels on the island. Tenerife receives more than 5 million tourists every year; of the Сanary islands Tenerife is the most popular.[33][131]

Currently, the municipality of [Adeje](/wiki/Adeje "Adeje") in the south of the island has the highest concentration of 5 star hotels in Europe[132] and also has what is considered the best luxury hotel in Spain according to _World Travel Awards_.[133]

### Agriculture and fishing

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=37 "Edit section: Agriculture and fishing")]

[](/wiki/File:Bananen_von_Teneriffa,_Spanien_\(48225526077\).jpg)[Banana plantations](/wiki/Banana_plantation "Banana plantation") in the western coastline ([Guía de Isora](/wiki/Gu%C3%ADa_de_Isora "Guía de Isora"))

Since tourism dominates the Tenerifan economy, the service sector is the largest. Industry and commerce contribute 40% of the non-tourist economy.[134] Agriculture contributes less than 10% of the island's GDP, but its contribution is vital as it also generates indirect benefits by maintaining the rural appearance of the island and supporting Tenerifan cultural values. 

Agriculture is centred on the northern slopes, and is affected by altitude as well as orientation: in the coastal zone, tomatoes and bananas are cultivated, these high yield products are for export to mainland Spain and the rest of Europe; in the drier intermediate zone, potatoes, tobacco and maize are grown, whilst in the south, onions are important.[33]

Bananas are a particularly important crop, as Tenerife grows more bananas than the other Canary Islands, with a current annual production of about 150,000 tons, down from the peak production of 200,000 tons in 1986. More than 90% of the total is destined for the international market, and banana growing occupies about 4200 hectares.[135] After the banana the most important crops are, in order of importance, tomatoes, grapes, potatoes and flowers. Fishing is also a major contributor to the Tenerifan economy, as the Canaries are Spain's second most important fishing grounds. 

### Energy

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=38 "Edit section: Energy")]

As of 2009, Tenerife had 910 MW of electrical generation capacity, which is mostly powered from petroleum-derived fuels. The island had 37 MW of wind turbines and 79 MW of solar panels.[136]

### Industry and commerce

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=39 "Edit section: Industry and commerce")]

Commerce in Tenerife plays a significant role in the economy, representing almost 20% of the GDP, with the commercial center Santa Cruz de Tenerife generating most of the earnings. Although there are a diversity of industrial estates that exist on the island, the most important industrial activity is petroleum, representing 10% of the island's GDP, again largely due to the capital Santa Cruz de Tenerife with its refinery. It provides petroleum products not only to the Canaries archipelago but is also an active in the markets of the Iberian Peninsula, Africa and South America. 

## Main sights

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=40 "Edit section: Main sights")]

### Monuments

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=41 "Edit section: Monuments")]

[](/wiki/File:Castillo_o_Torre_de_San_Andr%C3%A9s.jpg)[Castle of San Andrés](/wiki/Castle_of_San_Andr%C3%A9s "Castle of San Andrés"), declared of National Tourist Interest Center

Historical sights in the island, especially from the time after the conquest, include the [Cathedral of San Cristóbal de La Laguna](/wiki/Cathedral_of_La_Laguna "Cathedral of La Laguna"), the [Church of the Conception of La Laguna](/wiki/Iglesia_de_la_Concepci%C3%B3n_\(San_Crist%C3%B3bal_de_La_Laguna\) "Iglesia de la Concepción \(San Cristóbal de La Laguna\)") and the [Church of the Conception](/wiki/Iglesia_de_la_Concepci%C3%B3n_\(Santa_Cruz_de_Tenerife\) "Iglesia de la Concepción \(Santa Cruz de Tenerife\)") in the capital. The [Basílica de Nuestra Senora de la Candelaria](/wiki/Basilica_of_Candelaria "Basilica of Candelaria") can be found on the island (Patron of Canary Islands). Also on the island are the defensive castles located in the village of [San Andrés](/wiki/San_Andr%C3%A9s,_Santa_Cruz_de_Tenerife "San Andrés, Santa Cruz de Tenerife"), as well as many others throughout the island. 

The [Auditorio de Tenerife](/wiki/Auditorio_de_Tenerife "Auditorio de Tenerife"), one of the most modern in Spain, can be found at the entry port to the capital (in the southern part of Port of Santa Cruz de Tenerife). Near the Auditorio de Tenerife are El Castillo Negro and El Parque Marítimo. The [Torres de Santa Cruz](/wiki/Torres_de_Santa_Cruz "Torres de Santa Cruz") are two twin skyscrapers 120 metres (390 feet) high (the highest residential buildings in Spain and the tallest skyscrapers in the Canary Islands).[137]

### Archeological sites

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=42 "Edit section: Archeological sites")]

The island also has several [archaeological sites](/wiki/Archaeological_site "Archaeological site") of Guanche time (prior to the conquest), which generally are cave paintings that are scattered throughout the island, but most are found in the south of the island. 

Archaeological sites on the island include the [Cave of the Guanches](/wiki/Cave_of_the_Guanches "Cave of the Guanches"), where the oldest remains in the archipelago have been found,[138] dating to the 6th century BC,[139] and the [Caves of Don Gaspar](/wiki/Caves_of_Don_Gaspar "Caves of Don Gaspar"), where the finding of plant debris in the form of carbonized seeds indicates that the Guanches practiced agriculture on the island.[138] Both deposits are in the town of [Icod de los Vinos](/wiki/Icod_de_los_Vinos "Icod de los Vinos"). Also noteworthy on the island is the [Estación solar de Masca](/w/index.php?title=Estaci%C3%B3n_solar_de_Masca&action=edit&redlink=1 "Estación solar de Masca \(page does not exist\)") (_Masca Solar Station_), which was an aboriginal sanctuary for the celebration of rites related to fertility and the request for rainwater. This is located in the municipality of [Buenavista del Norte](/wiki/Buenavista_del_Norte "Buenavista del Norte"). 

Other archaeological sites include that of Los Cambados and that of El Barranco del Rey both in [Arona](/wiki/Arona,_Tenerife "Arona, Tenerife").[140] One could also highlight the [Cueva de Achbinico](/wiki/Cueva_de_Achbinico "Cueva de Achbinico") (first shrine Marian of the Canary Islands, Guanche vintage-Spanish). In addition there are some buildings called [Güímar Pyramids](/wiki/G%C3%BC%C3%ADmar_Pyramids "Güímar Pyramids"), whose origin is uncertain. 

There are also traces that reveal the [Punic](/wiki/Punic "Punic") presence on the island, as in the wake commonly called "[Stone of the Guanches](/wiki/Stone_of_the_Guanches "Stone of the Guanches")" in the town of [Taganana](/wiki/Taganana "Taganana"). This archaeological site consists of a structure formed by a stone block, large, outdoor, featuring rock carvings on its surface. Among these is the presence of a representation of the [Carthaginian](/wiki/Ancient_Carthage "Ancient Carthage") goddess [Tanit](/wiki/Tanit "Tanit"),[141] represented by a bottle-shaped symbol surrounded by cruciform motifs. It is thought that the monument was originally an altar of sacrifice linked to those found in the [Semitic](/wiki/Ancient_Semitic_religion "Ancient Semitic religion")[141] field and then reused for [Aboriginal ritual of mummification](/wiki/Guanche_mummies "Guanche mummies").[141]

## Culture and arts

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=43 "Edit section: Culture and arts")]

### Literature

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=44 "Edit section: Literature")]

In the 16th and 17th centuries, Antonio de Viana, a native of La Laguna, composed the epic poem _Antigüedades de las Islas Afortunadas_ (_Antiquities of the Fortunate Isles_), a work of value to anthropologists, since it sheds light on Canarian life of the time.[142] The Enlightenment reached Tenerife, and literary and artistic figures of this era include [José Viera y Clavijo](/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Viera_y_Clavijo "José Viera y Clavijo"), [Tomás de Iriarte y Oropesa](/wiki/Tom%C3%A1s_de_Iriarte_y_Oropesa "Tomás de Iriarte y Oropesa"), [Ángel Guimerá y Jorge](/wiki/%C3%80ngel_Guimer%C3%A0 "Àngel Guimerà"), [Mercedes Pinto](/wiki/Mercedes_Pinto "Mercedes Pinto") and [Domingo Pérez Minik](/wiki/Domingo_P%C3%A9rez_Minik "Domingo Pérez Minik"), amongst others. 

### Painting

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=45 "Edit section: Painting")]

[](/wiki/File:Tomas_de_Iriarte_Joaquin_Inza.jpg)[Tomás de Iriarte y Oropesa](/wiki/Tom%C3%A1s_de_Iriarte_y_Oropesa "Tomás de Iriarte y Oropesa")

During the course of the 16th century, several painters flourished in La Laguna, as well as in other places on the island, including [Garachico](/wiki/Garachico "Garachico"), Santa Cruz de Tenerife, La Orotava and [Puerto de la Cruz](/wiki/Puerto_de_la_Cruz "Puerto de la Cruz"). Cristóbal Hernández de Quintana and Gaspar de Quevedo, considered the best Canarian painters of the 17th century, were natives of La Orotava, and their art can be found in churches on Tenerife.[143]

The work of [Luis de la Cruz y Ríos](/w/index.php?title=Luis_de_la_Cruz_y_R%C3%ADos&action=edit&redlink=1 "Luis de la Cruz y Ríos \(page does not exist\)") can be found in the church of Nuestra Señora de la Peña de Francia, in Puerto de la Cruz. Born in 1775, he became court painter to [Ferdinand VII of Spain](/wiki/Ferdinand_VII_of_Spain "Ferdinand VII of Spain") and was also a miniaturist, and achieved a favorable position in the royal court. He was known there by the nickname of "El Canario."[144]

The landscape painter Valentín Sanz (born 1849) was a native of Santa Cruz de Tenerife, and the _Museo Municipal de Bellas Artes de Santa Cruz_ displays many of his works. This museum also contains the works of [Juan Rodríguez Botas](/wiki/Juan_Rodr%C3%ADguez_Botas "Juan Rodríguez Botas") (1880–1917), considered the first Canarian [impressionist](/wiki/Impressionist "Impressionist").[145]

Frescoes by the [expressionist](/wiki/Expressionist "Expressionist") Mariano de Cossío can be found in the church of Santo Domingo, in La Laguna. The watercolorist Francisco Bonnín Guerín (born 1874) was a native of Santa Cruz, and founded a school to encourage the arts. [Óscar Domínguez](/wiki/%C3%93scar_Dom%C3%ADnguez "Óscar Domínguez") was born in La Laguna in 1906 and is famed for his versatility. He belonged to the [surrealist](/wiki/Surrealist "Surrealist") school, and invented the technique known as _[decalcomania](/wiki/Decalcomania "Decalcomania")_.[146]

### Sculpture

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=46 "Edit section: Sculpture")]

The arrival from Seville of [Martín de Andújar Cantos](/wiki/Mart%C3%ADn_de_And%C3%BAjar_Cantos "Martín de Andújar Cantos"), an architect and sculptor brought new sculpting techniques of the Seville school, which were passed down to his students, including [Blas García Ravelo](/wiki/Blas_Garc%C3%ADa_Ravelo "Blas García Ravelo"), a native of Garachico. He had been trained by the master sculptor [Juan Martínez Montañés](/wiki/Juan_Mart%C3%ADnez_Monta%C3%B1%C3%A9s "Juan Martínez Montañés").[147]

Other notable sculptors from the 17th and 18th centuries include [Sebastián Fernández Méndez](/wiki/Sebasti%C3%A1n_Fern%C3%A1ndez_M%C3%A9ndez "Sebastián Fernández Méndez"), [Lázaro González de Ocampo](/wiki/L%C3%A1zaro_Gonz%C3%A1lez_de_Ocampo "Lázaro González de Ocampo"), [José Rodríguez de la Oliva](/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Rodr%C3%ADguez_de_la_Oliva "José Rodríguez de la Oliva"), and most importantly, [Fernando Estévez](/wiki/Fernando_Est%C3%A9vez_\(sculptor\) "Fernando Estévez \(sculptor\)"), a native of La Orotava and a student of Luján Pérez. Estévez contributed an extensive collection of religious images and woodcarvings, found in numerous churches of Tenerife, such as the Principal Parish of [Saint James the Great](/wiki/Saint_James_the_Great "Saint James the Great") (_Parroquia Matriz del Apóstol Santiago_), in [Los Realejos](/wiki/Los_Realejos "Los Realejos"); in the Cathedral of La Laguna; the _Iglesia de la Concepción_ in La Laguna; the basilica of [Candelaria](/wiki/Candelaria,_Tenerife "Candelaria, Tenerife"), and various churches in La Orotava. 

### Music

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=47 "Edit section: Music")]

[](/wiki/File:Timple_Front.png)Canarian timple

An important musician from Tenerife is Teobaldo Power y Lugo Viña, a native of Santa Cruz and a pianist and composer, and author of the _Cantos Canarios_.[148] The Hymn of the Canary Islands takes its melody from the _Arrorró_ , or Lullaby, from Power y Lugo Viña's _Cantos Canarios_.[149]

Folkloric music has also flourished on the island, and, as in the rest of the islands, is characterized by the use of the [Canarian Timple](/wiki/Timple "Timple"), the guitar, [bandurria](/wiki/Bandurria "Bandurria"), [laúd](/wiki/La%C3%BAd "Laúd"), and various percussion instruments. Local folkloric groups such as [Los Sabandeños](/wiki/Los_Sabande%C3%B1os "Los Sabandeños") work to save Tenerife's musical forms in the face of increasing cultural pressure from the mainland.[150]

Tenerife is the home to the types of songs called the _isa_ , _folía_ , _tajaraste_ , and _malagueña_ , which are a cross of ancient Guanche songs and those of [Andalusia](/wiki/Andalusia "Andalusia") and Latin America. 

### Architecture

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=48 "Edit section: Architecture")]

[](/wiki/File:G%C3%BC%C3%ADmar_BW_2.JPG)[Pyramids of Güímar](/wiki/Pyramids_of_G%C3%BC%C3%ADmar "Pyramids of Güímar") [](/wiki/File:Tenerife2005_056.jpg)Architecture in Santa Cruz ([Plaza de España](/wiki/Plaza_de_Espa%C3%B1a_\(Santa_Cruz_de_Tenerife\) "Plaza de España \(Santa Cruz de Tenerife\)")) [](/wiki/File:Auditorio_de_Tenerife_013.JPG)[Auditorio de Tenerife](/wiki/Auditorio_de_Tenerife "Auditorio de Tenerife"), icon of architecture in Canary Islands[151]

Tenerife is characterized by an architecture whose best representatives are the local manor houses and also the most humble and common dwellings. This style, while influenced by those of Andalusia and Portugal, nevertheless had a very particular and native character.[53]

Of the manor houses, the best examples can be found in La Orotava and in La Laguna, characterized by their balconies and by the existence of interior patios and the widespread use of the wood known as _pino tea_ ("[pitch pine](/wiki/Pitch_pine "Pitch pine")"). These houses are characterized by simple façades and wooden lattices with little ornamentation.[53] There are sash windows and it is customary for the chairs inside the house to rest back-to-back to the windows. The interior patios function like real gardens that serve to give extra light to the rooms, which are connected via the patio by galleries frequently crowned by wood and stone. 

Gadgets like stills, water pumps, benches and counters, are elements that frequently form part of these patios.[53]

Traditional houses generally have two storeys, with rough walls of variegated colours. Sometimes the continuity of these walls is interrupted by the presence of stone blocks that are used for ornamental purposes.[53]

The government buildings and religious structures were built according to the changing styles of each century. The urban nuclei of La Orotava and La Laguna have been declared national historical-artistic monuments.[152]

In recent years, various governments have spearheaded the concept of developing architectural projects, sometimes ostentatious ones, designed by renowned architects–for example, the remodeling of the [Plaza de España](/wiki/Plaza_de_Espa%C3%B1a_\(Santa_Cruz_de_Tenerife\) "Plaza de España \(Santa Cruz de Tenerife\)") in Santa Cruz de Tenerife by the Swiss architects [Herzog & de Meuron](/wiki/Herzog_%26_de_Meuron "Herzog & de Meuron"). Other examples include the Playa de Las Teresitas project by the Frenchman Dominique Perrault; the center known as Magma Arte & Congresos; the Torres de Santa Cruz; and the [Auditorio de Tenerife](/wiki/Auditorio_de_Tenerife "Auditorio de Tenerife") ("Auditorium of Tenerife"). The latter, by the Spaniard [Santiago Calatrava](/wiki/Santiago_Calatrava "Santiago Calatrava"), lies to the east of the Parque Marítimo ("Maritime Park"), in Santa Cruz de Tenerife, and is characterized by its sail-like structure, which evokes a boat, and has become a symbol for the city and island,[153] which makes Santa Cruz de Tenerife one of the Spanish cities with the most futuristic buildings. 

### Crafts

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=49 "Edit section: Crafts")]

[](/wiki/File:Traje_t%C3%ADpico.JPG)Traditional costume

Distinctive representatives of craftsmanship on the island are [Tenerife lace](/wiki/Tenerife_lace "Tenerife lace") (_calado canario_), which is drawn work [embroidery](/wiki/Embroidery "Embroidery"), and the intricate [doilies](/wiki/Doilies "Doilies") known as _rosetas_ , or [rosette](/wiki/Rosette_\(design\) "Rosette \(design\)") embroidery, particularly from [Vilaflor](/wiki/Vilaflor "Vilaflor"). The lace, often made for table linen, is produced by the intricate and slow embroidering of a stretched piece of cloth, which is rigidly attached to a wooden frame and is finished with illustrations or patterns using threads that are crossed over and wound around the _fijadores_ , or pins stuck in a small support made of cloth.[154] These decorated, small pieces are afterwards joined, to produce distinct designs and pieces of cloth.[155]

Another Tenerife-based industry is cabinetwork. The north of the island produced various master craftsman who created distinctive balconies, celosias, doors, and windows, as well as furniture consisting of pieces made in fine wood. Basketmaking using palm-leaves was also an important industry. Other materials are chestnut tree branches stripped of their leaves and [banana tree](/wiki/Banana_tree "Banana tree") fibre (known locally as _la badana_).[156]

Pottery has a long history harking back to the production of ceramics by the Guanches. The Guanches were unfamiliar with the [potter's wheel](/wiki/Potter%27s_wheel "Potter's wheel"), and used hand-worked clay, which gave their pottery a distinctive look. Pottery was used to produce domestic objects such as pots and grills, or ornamental pieces such as bead collars or the objects known as _pintaderas_ , which were pieces of pottery used to decorate other vessels.[33]

### Traditional celebrations

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=50 "Edit section: Traditional celebrations")]

[](/wiki/File:BajadaSocorro2006.jpg)Annual performance to honour "Our Lady of Candelaria" at Socorro Beach, Güímar

#### Carnival of Santa Cruz

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=51 "Edit section: Carnival of Santa Cruz")]

Perhaps the most important festival of Tenerife, popular both on a national and international level, is the [Carnival of Santa Cruz de Tenerife](/wiki/Carnival_of_Santa_Cruz_de_Tenerife "Carnival of Santa Cruz de Tenerife"), which has been declared a Festival of International Tourist Interest (_Fiesta de Interés Turístico Internacional_).[157] The carnival is celebrated in many locations in the north and south of the island, but is largest in scope in the city of Santa Cruz.[158] Contests are celebrated, and the carnival includes bands of street musicians (_murgas_), groups of minstrels (_rondallas de Tenerife_), masquerades (_comparsas_), and various associations (_agrupaciones_). Once the Queen of the festival is elected, the first part of the carnival ends, and thereafter begins the actual street carnival, in which large numbers of people gather in the centre of Santa Cruz, with the carnival lasting ten days.[159]

#### Pilgrimages (_Romerías_)

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=52 "Edit section: Pilgrimages \(Romerías\)")]

The most traditional and widespread religious festivals on the islands are the pilgrimages or _romerías_.[160] These events, which incorporate Christian and non-Christian elements, are celebrated by various means: with wagons and [floats](/wiki/Float_\(parade\) "Float \(parade\)"), plowing teams and livestock, in honor of the [patron saint](/wiki/Patron_saint "Patron saint") of a particular place. The processions are accompanied by local dances, local dishes, folkloric activities, local arts and crafts, local sports, and the wearing of traditional dress of Tenerife (_trajes de mago_). 

The origins of these events can be attributed to the parties and celebrations held by the richest classes of the island, who would gather to venerate their patron saints, to which they attributed good harvests, fertile lands, plentiful rainfall, the curing of sicknesses and ending of epidemics, etc. They would thus give homage to these saints by consuming and sharing the fruits of their harvest, which included the locally cultivated wines. These have developed into processions to mark festivals dedicated to [Saint Mark](/wiki/Saint_Mark "Saint Mark") in [Tegueste](/wiki/Tegueste "Tegueste"), where the wagons are decorated with the fruits of the earth (seeds, cereals, flowers, etc.); to [Saint Isidore the Laborer](/wiki/Saint_Isidore_the_Laborer "Saint Isidore the Laborer") in Los Realejos; to Saint Isidore the Laborer and [Maria Torribia](/wiki/Maria_Torribia "Maria Torribia") (_Saint Mary of the Head_) in La Orotava; the [Romería Regional de San Benito Abad](/wiki/Romer%C3%ADa_Regional_de_San_Benito_Abad "Romería Regional de San Benito Abad") in La Laguna; [Virgin of Candelaria](/wiki/Virgin_of_Candelaria "Virgin of Candelaria") in Candelaria; [Saint Roch](/wiki/Saint_Roch "Saint Roch") in Garachico; [Saint Augustine](/wiki/Saint_Augustine "Saint Augustine") in [Arafo](/wiki/Arafo "Arafo"); and the [Romería del Socorro](/wiki/Romer%C3%ADa_del_Socorro "Romería del Socorro") in Güímar. 

#### Holiday of the Virgin of Candelaria

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=53 "Edit section: Holiday of the Virgin of Candelaria")]

The [Virgin of Candelaria](/wiki/Virgin_of_Candelaria "Virgin of Candelaria") is the patron of the Canary Islands; a feast is held in her honor two times a year, in February and August. The [Romeria](/wiki/Romeria "Romeria")-Offering to the Virgin of Candelaria is celebrated every 15 August in this event is a tradition that representations of all municipalities of the island and also of all the Canary archipelago come to make offerings to their patron. Another significant act of the feast of the Virgin of Candelaria is called "_Walk to Candelaria_ " held on the night of 14 to 15 August in which the [faithful make pilgrimage on foot from various parts of the island](/wiki/Pilgrimage_to_Candelaria "Pilgrimage to Candelaria"), even coming from other islands to arrive at [Villa Mariana de Candelaria](/wiki/Candelaria,_Tenerife "Candelaria, Tenerife"). 

On 2 February we celebrate the feast of the Candelaria. Also on this day come to town many devotees of the Virgin. During the February festivities, the so-called "Procesión de Las Candelas" (_Candlelight Procession_) stands out, in which the faithful accompany the Virgin in the dark of the night lit only by candles and praying the rosary. 

#### Holiday of the Cristo de La Laguna

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=54 "Edit section: Holiday of the Cristo de La Laguna")]

It is celebrated every 14 September in honor of a much venerated image of Christ in the Archipelago, the [Cristo de La Laguna](/wiki/Cristo_de_La_Laguna "Cristo de La Laguna"), is held in the city of [San Cristóbal de La Laguna](/wiki/San_Crist%C3%B3bal_de_La_Laguna "San Cristóbal de La Laguna"). Every 9 September, the venerated image of Christ is lowered in public from the main altar of its Royal Sanctuary, after which, the faithful kiss the feet of the image. The image is solemnly transferred in procession to the Cathedral of La Laguna on the afternoon of that day, where it remains for several days, until 14 September, which is the main day, when it is transferred back to its Sanctuary. 

#### Corpus Christi

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=55 "Edit section: Corpus Christi")]

[](/wiki/File:Plaza-centro.jpg)Soil Tapestry in the Plaza del Ayuntamiento (Town Square) in [La Orotava](/wiki/La_Orotava "La Orotava")

The religious festival of [Corpus Christi](/wiki/Corpus_Christi_\(feast\) "Corpus Christi \(feast\)") is particularly important, and is traditionally celebrated with floral carpets laid in the streets. Particularly noteworthy are the celebrations in La Orotava where a very large carpet, or tapestry, of different coloured volcanic soils, covers the Plaza del Ayuntamiento (town square). These soils are taken from the [Parque Nacional del Teide](/wiki/Parque_Nacional_del_Teide "Parque Nacional del Teide"), and after the celebration, are returned, to preserve the National Park. The celebration of Corpus Christi in Orotava has been declared of Important Cultural Interest among the official Traditional Activities of the Island.[161]

#### Easter

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=56 "Edit section: Easter")]

Among the numerous other celebrations that define Tenerifan culture, Easter remains the most important. This is celebrated across the island, but is particularly notable in the municipalities of La Laguna, La Orotava and Los Realejos, where elaborate processions take place on Maundy Thursday, Good Friday and Easter Sunday, or "Resurrection Sunday". Holy Week in the city of San Cristobal de la Laguna is the largest of the Canary Islands.[162]

## Religion

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=57 "Edit section: Religion")]

[](/wiki/File:La_Laguna_2022_-_main_facade.jpg)[Cathedral of La Laguna](/wiki/Cathedral_of_La_Laguna "Cathedral of La Laguna"), seat of the local Catholic diocese

As with the rest of Spain, Tenerife is largely [Catholic](/wiki/Catholic_Church "Catholic Church").[163] However, the practice of other religions and denominations has increasingly expanded on the island due to tourism and immigration, as [Islam](/wiki/Islam "Islam"), [Hinduism](/wiki/Hinduism "Hinduism"), [Buddhism](/wiki/Buddhism "Buddhism"), [Evangelicalism](/wiki/Evangelicalism "Evangelicalism"), [Judaism](/wiki/Judaism "Judaism") and [Afro-American religion](/wiki/Afro-American_religion "Afro-American religion").[164] Minority religions are stationed in the island: [Chinese Religions](/wiki/Chinese_Religions "Chinese Religions"),[165] [Baháʼí](/wiki/Bah%C3%A1%CA%BC%C3%AD_Faith "Baháʼí Faith")[165] and the neopaganism native form, the [Church of the Guanche People](/wiki/Church_of_the_Guanche_People "Church of the Guanche People"),[165] among others. Christianity and Judaism arrived in the island with the Spanish conquest. 

[](/wiki/File:Tenerife.candelaria.exterior.basilica.jpg)[Basilica of Candelaria](/wiki/Basilica_of_Candelaria "Basilica of Candelaria"), sanctuary of the [Virgin of Candelaria](/wiki/Virgin_of_Candelaria "Virgin of Candelaria"), patron saint of the Canary Islands

An important Catholic festival is the celebration of the [feast day](/wiki/Feast_day "Feast day") associated with the [Virgin of Candelaria](/wiki/Virgin_of_Candelaria "Virgin of Candelaria"), [patron saint](/wiki/Patron_saint "Patron saint") of the Canary Islands, who represents the union of the Guanche and Spanish cultures.[166] The Guanches became devoted to a [Black Madonna](/wiki/Black_Madonna "Black Madonna") that Christian missionaries from [Lanzarote](/wiki/Lanzarote "Lanzarote") and [Fuerteventura](/wiki/Fuerteventura "Fuerteventura") left on a beach near the present-day Villa Mariana de Candelaria, which gave rise to the legends and stories associated with the Virgin. These legends fueled the cult of the Virgin and the [pilgrimages to Candelaria](/wiki/Pilgrimage_to_Candelaria "Pilgrimage to Candelaria") that have existed to this day on the island. Another cult to the Virgin Mary exists in the form of [Our Lady of Los Remedios](/wiki/Our_Lady_of_Los_Remedios "Our Lady of Los Remedios") (_la Virgen de Los Remedios_), who is patron of the island and Catholic diocese of Tenerife (_Diócesis Nivariense_). 

[](/wiki/File:Hermano_Pedro.jpg)[Peter of Saint Joseph de Betancur](/wiki/Peter_of_Saint_Joseph_de_Betancur "Peter of Saint Joseph de Betancur"), [franciscan](/wiki/Franciscan "Franciscan") missionary in [Guatemala](/wiki/Guatemala "Guatemala") born in Tenerife. He was the first Canarian to be canonized by the [Catholic Church](/wiki/Catholic_Church "Catholic Church"). This saint is very venerated in Tenerife.

In Tenerife were born two [Catholic](/wiki/Catholic_Church "Catholic Church") [saints](/wiki/Saint "Saint") who were of the greatest missionaries in the Americas: [Peter of Saint Joseph Betancur](/wiki/Peter_of_Saint_Joseph_Betancur "Peter of Saint Joseph Betancur") and [José de Anchieta](/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_de_Anchieta "José de Anchieta"). The first was a missionary in [Guatemala](/wiki/Guatemala "Guatemala") and founder of [Order of Our Lady of Bethlehem](/wiki/Bethlehemites "Bethlehemites") (the first religious order of the Americas), the second was a missionary in Brazil, and was one of the founders of [São Paulo](/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo "São Paulo") and of [Rio de Janeiro](/wiki/Rio_de_Janeiro "Rio de Janeiro"). It also highlights the figure of the mystic [Mary of Jesus de León y Delgado](/wiki/Mary_of_Jesus_de_Le%C3%B3n_y_Delgado "Mary of Jesus de León y Delgado"). This nun died with a reputation for holiness and is highly revered throughout the Canary Islands. Her body is intact in the Convent of Santa Catalina de Siena in [San Cristóbal de La Laguna](/wiki/San_Crist%C3%B3bal_de_La_Laguna "San Cristóbal de La Laguna"). 

Principal Catholic places of worship on the island include: 

  * _The[Basilica of Candelaria](/wiki/Basilica_of_Candelaria "Basilica of Candelaria")_ (in [Candelaria](/wiki/Candelaria,_Tenerife "Candelaria, Tenerife")): The place where the image of the Virgin of Candelaria can be found, this sanctuary is built in [neoclassical](/wiki/Neoclassical_architecture "Neoclassical architecture") style, and is visited daily by the parishioners, who visit the Villa Mariana out of devotion to the Virgin.
  * The _[Cathedral of La Laguna](/wiki/Cathedral_of_La_Laguna "Cathedral of La Laguna")_ (in [San Cristóbal de La Laguna](/wiki/San_Crist%C3%B3bal_de_La_Laguna "San Cristóbal de La Laguna")): The seat of the Diocese of Tenerife (known as the Diócesis Nivariense, or Nivarian Diocese), the cathedral is dedicated to Our Lady of Remedies (_la Virgen de Los Remedios_). A combination of [neo-Gothic](/wiki/Neo-Gothic "Neo-Gothic") and neoclassical architectural elements.
  * _[Real Santuario del Cristo de La Laguna](/wiki/Real_Santuario_del_Cristo_de_La_Laguna "Real Santuario del Cristo de La Laguna")_ (in San Cristóbal de La Laguna): One of the most important churches in the Canary Islands, it contains the venerated image of the [Cristo de La Laguna](/wiki/Cristo_de_La_Laguna "Cristo de La Laguna"), and is a symbol of the city of San Cristóbal de La Laguna.
  * _*[Mother Parish of the Apostle Santiago (Los Realejos)](/wiki/Mother_Parish_of_the_Apostle_Santiago_\(Los_Realejos\) "Mother Parish of the Apostle Santiago \(Los Realejos\)")_ (_Parroquia Matriz del Apóstol Santiago_): Situated in [Villa de Los Realejos](/wiki/Los_Realejos "Los Realejos"), this parish church was the first Christian church built on the island after its conquest by [Castilian](/wiki/Kingdom_of_Castile "Kingdom of Castile") forces. It is dedicated to [Saint James the Great](/wiki/Saint_James_the_Great "Saint James the Great"), as the _conquista_ was completed on the saint's [feast day](/wiki/Feast_day "Feast day") on 25 July 1496. It was, along with the Parish of the Conception of La Laguna, one of the first parishes of the island.
  * _[The Church of the Conception of La Laguna](/wiki/Iglesia_de_la_Concepci%C3%B3n_\(San_Crist%C3%B3bal_de_La_Laguna\) "Iglesia de la Concepción \(San Cristóbal de La Laguna\)")_ (_Iglesia de la Concepción de La Laguna_): One of the most ancient buildings on Tenerife, its construction was ordered by [Alonso Fernández de Lugo](/wiki/Alonso_Fern%C3%A1ndez_de_Lugo "Alonso Fernández de Lugo"). It has been declared a National Historic Monument. Around this church were built the dwellings and infrastructure that formed the nucleus of the city of San Cristóbal de La Laguna.



Other important churches include the Church of the Conception in La Orotava (Iglesia de la Concepción); the churches of San Agustín and Santo Domingo in La Orotava; the church of Nuestra Señora de la Peña de Francia in Puerto de la Cruz; the church of San Marcos in Icod de los Vinos; the church of Santa Ana in Garachico; and the [Church of the Conception (Iglesia de la Concepción) in Santa Cruz de Tenerife](/wiki/Iglesia_de_la_Concepci%C3%B3n_\(Santa_Cruz_de_Tenerife\) "Iglesia de la Concepción \(Santa Cruz de Tenerife\)"). 

The first saint of Tenerife[167] and Canary Islands[168] was [Saint Peter of Saint Joseph Betancur](/wiki/Peter_of_Saint_Joseph_Betancur "Peter of Saint Joseph Betancur"), born in the town of [Vilaflor](/wiki/Vilaflor "Vilaflor"), Tenerife. His main shrine is the [Cave of Santo Hermano Pedro](/wiki/Cave_of_Santo_Hermano_Pedro "Cave of Santo Hermano Pedro") in [Granadilla de Abona](/wiki/Granadilla_de_Abona "Granadilla de Abona"), near the coast, where he lived in his youth. This cave is considered one of the most important pilgrimage spots of the Canary Islands. 

Another notable building on the island is the [Masonic Temple of Santa Cruz de Tenerife](/wiki/Masonic_Temple_of_Santa_Cruz_de_Tenerife "Masonic Temple of Santa Cruz de Tenerife"), generally considered the finest example of Masonic temple architecture in Spain;[169] it was the Masonic center of the country until the military occupation of the island by the [Franco regime](/wiki/Franco_regime "Franco regime").[170]

The headquarters of the [Islamic Federation of the Canary Islands](/wiki/Islamic_Federation_of_the_Canary_Islands "Islamic Federation of the Canary Islands") is in Tenerife; the organization was founded to unite the Muslim communities of the Canary Islands in a common association.[171] For its part, the headquarters of the Evangelical Council of the Canary Islands is also on the island.[172]

## Education

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=58 "Edit section: Education")]

[Formal education](/wiki/Formal_education "Formal education") in Tenerife began with the [religious orders](/wiki/Religious_order "Religious order"). In 1530, the [Dominican Order](/wiki/Dominican_Order "Dominican Order") established a chair of philosophy at the convent of [La Concepción de La Laguna](/wiki/Iglesia_de_la_Concepci%C3%B3n_\(San_Crist%C3%B3bal_de_La_Laguna\) "Iglesia de la Concepción \(San Cristóbal de La Laguna\)"). Still, until well into the 18th century Tenerife was largely without institutions of education. 

[](/wiki/File:UniversityLaLaguna.JPG)[University of La Laguna](/wiki/University_of_La_Laguna "University of La Laguna"), the oldest and largest university in the Canary Islands

Such institutions finally began to develop thanks to the work of the Real Sociedad Económica de Amigos del País ("Royal Economic Society of Friends of the Country"), which established several schools in San Cristóbal de La Laguna. The first of these was an institute of secondary education established in 1846 to fill the gap left by the closure of the Universidad de San Fernando (_see[University of La Laguna](/wiki/University_of_La_Laguna "University of La Laguna")_).[173]

An 1850 annex to this building was the Escuela Normal Elemental, the archipelago's first teachers' college or normal school, which became the Escuela Normal Superior de Magisterio from 1866 onward. These were the only institutions of higher education until the dictator [Miguel Primo de Rivera](/wiki/Miguel_Primo_de_Rivera "Miguel Primo de Rivera") created several institutions. A turning point came around the time of the rise of the [Second Spanish Republic](/wiki/Second_Spanish_Republic "Second Spanish Republic"). From 1929 to 1933 the number of schools nearly doubled. 

Shortly after this, though, the start of the [Spanish Civil War](/wiki/Spanish_Civil_War "Spanish Civil War") and the following dictatorship of [Francisco Franco](/wiki/Francisco_Franco "Francisco Franco") constituted a considerable reversal. Education in the hands of religious orders had a certain importance on the island until the 1970 Ley General de Educación ("General Law of Education") shifted the balance from religiously based education to public education. Public schools continued their advance during and after the post-Franco [Spanish transition to democracy](/wiki/Spanish_transition_to_democracy "Spanish transition to democracy"). Tenerife today has 301 centers of childhood education ([preschools](/wiki/Preschool "Preschool")), 297 primary schools, 140 secondary schools and 86 [post-secondary](/wiki/Post-secondary_education "Post-secondary education") schools.[174] There are also five universities or [post-graduate](/wiki/Post-graduate_education "Post-graduate education") schools, the [University of La Laguna](/wiki/University_of_La_Laguna "University of La Laguna"), the [Universidad Nacional de Educación a Distancia](/wiki/Universidad_Nacional_de_Educaci%C3%B3n_a_Distancia "Universidad Nacional de Educación a Distancia") (National University of Distance Learning), the [Universidad Internacional Menéndez Pelayo](/wiki/Universidad_Internacional_Men%C3%A9ndez_Pelayo "Universidad Internacional Menéndez Pelayo") (Menéndez Pelayo International University), the [Universidad Alfonso X el Sabio](/w/index.php?title=Universidad_Alfonso_X_el_Sabio&action=edit&redlink=1 "Universidad Alfonso X el Sabio \(page does not exist\)") (University of Alfonso X the Wise) and the [Universidad de Vic](/w/index.php?title=Universidad_de_Vic&action=edit&redlink=1 "Universidad de Vic \(page does not exist\)") (Escuela Universitaria de Turismo de Santa Cruz de Tenerife, "University School of Tourism of Santa Cruz de Tenerife"). The largest of these is the University of La Laguna. 

The [Universidad Europea de Canarias](/wiki/Universidad_Europea_de_Canarias "Universidad Europea de Canarias") (European University of the Canary Islands) is located in La Orotava and is the first private university in the Canary Islands.[175]

### Science and research

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=59 "Edit section: Science and research")]

[](/wiki/File:Obs_2.jpg)[Teide Observatory](/wiki/Teide_Observatory "Teide Observatory"), part of the [Instituto de Astrofísica de Canarias](/wiki/Instituto_de_Astrof%C3%ADsica_de_Canarias "Instituto de Astrofísica de Canarias") ([Astrophysics](/wiki/Astrophysics "Astrophysics") Institute of the Canaries)

While Tenerife is not prominent in the history of scientific and academic research, it is the home of the [Instituto de Astrofísica de Canarias](/wiki/Instituto_de_Astrof%C3%ADsica_de_Canarias "Instituto de Astrofísica de Canarias") (Astrophysical Institute of the Canaries). There is also an Instituto de Bio-Orgánica [Antonio González](/wiki/Antonio_Gonz%C3%A1lez_Gonz%C3%A1lez "Antonio González González") (Antonio González Bio-Organic Institute) at the University of La Laguna. Also at that university are the Instituto de Lingüística Andrés Bello (Andrés Bello Institute of Linguistics), the Centro de Estudios Medievales y Renacentistas (Center for Medieval and Renaissance Studies), the Instituto Universitario de la Empresa (University Institute of Business), the Instituto de Derecho Regional (Regional Institute of Law), the Instituto Universitario de Ciencias Políticas y Sociales (University Institute of Political and Social Sciences) and the Instituto de Enfermedades Tropicales (Institute of Tropical Diseases). This last is one of the seven institutions of the Red de Investigación de Centros de Enfermedades Tropicales ([RICET](/w/index.php?title=RICET&action=edit&redlink=1 "RICET \(page does not exist\)"), "Network of Research of Centers of Tropical Diseases"), located in various parts of Spain. 

Puerto de la Cruz has the Instituto de Estudos Hispánicos de Canarias (Institute of Hispanic Studies of the Canaries), attached to Madrid's Instituto de Cultura Hispánica. In La Laguna is the Canarian delegation of the [Consejo Superior de Investigaciones Científicas](/wiki/Consejo_Superior_de_Investigaciones_Cient%C3%ADficas "Consejo Superior de Investigaciones Científicas") (CSIC, Superior Council of Scientific Investigations), the Instituto Canario de Investigaciones Agrarias (Canarian Institute of Agrarian Investigation), the Instituto de Estudios Canarios (Canarian Institute of Studies) and the [Centro Internacional para la Conservación del Patrimonio](/w/index.php?title=Centro_Internacional_para_la_Conservaci%C3%B3n_del_Patrimonio&action=edit&redlink=1 "Centro Internacional para la Conservación del Patrimonio \(page does not exist\)") (the International Center of the Conservation of Patrimony). 

Other research facilities in Tenerife are the Instituto Tecnológico de Canarias, the Instituto Vulcanológico de Canarias, the Asociación Industrial de Canarias, the Instituto Tecnológico de Energías Renovables (Technological Institute of Renewable Energy) and the Instituto Oceanográfico de Canarias in Santa Cruz de Tenerife. 

### Museums

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=60 "Edit section: Museums")]

[](/wiki/File:Momia_guanche_museo_santa_cruz_27-07.JPG)[Guanche mummy](/wiki/Guanche_mummies "Guanche mummies") in the [Museo de la Naturaleza y el Hombre](/wiki/Museo_de_la_Naturaleza_y_el_Hombre "Museo de la Naturaleza y el Hombre") [](/wiki/File:Pueblo_Chico_Guanchen2.jpg)[Guanche](/wiki/Guanches "Guanches") figures at Pueblo Chico in [La Oratava](/wiki/La_Oratava "La Oratava")

The island boasts a variety of museums of different natures, under dominion of a variety of institutions. Perhaps the most developed are those belonging to the [Organismo Autónomo de Museos y Centros](/wiki/Museos_de_Tenerife "Museos de Tenerife"),[176] which include the following: 

  * [Museum of Nature and Man](/wiki/Museo_de_la_Naturaleza_y_el_Hombre "Museo de la Naturaleza y el Hombre"): located in Santa Cruz de Tenerife, this museum exhibits the natural riches of the Canary Islands and of the pre-Hispanic people who inhabited these. The Museum of Nature and Man is a world reference in regard to preservation of [mummies](/wiki/Mummies "Mummies"). The complex is composed of three museums: 
    * The Museum of Natural Sciences
    * The Archaeological Museum of Tenerife
    * The Canarian Institute of Bioanthropology
  * [Museum of the History of Tenerife](/wiki/Museum_of_the_History_of_Tenerife "Museum of the History of Tenerife"): located in the city of [La Laguna](/wiki/San_Crist%C3%B3bal_de_La_Laguna "San Cristóbal de La Laguna"), the history of museum presents an overview of the institutional, social, economic and cultural development of the Island in from the 15th to 20th centuries.
  * The [Museum of Science and the Cosmos](/wiki/Museum_of_Science_and_the_Cosmos "Museum of Science and the Cosmos"), also located in La Laguna adjacent to the property of the Instituto de Astrofísica as a museum about the laws and principles of nature, from those of the cosmos to those of the human body.
  * The [Museum of Anthropology of Tenerife](/w/index.php?title=Museum_of_Anthropology_of_Tenerife&action=edit&redlink=1 "Museum of Anthropology of Tenerife \(page does not exist\)"), in La Laguna as well, more specifically in [Valle de Guerra](/w/index.php?title=Valle_de_Guerra&action=edit&redlink=1 "Valle de Guerra \(page does not exist\)") is a public institution for the investigation, conservation and spread of popular culture
  * The [Centro de Documentación Canario-Americano](/w/index.php?title=Centro_de_Documentaci%C3%B3n_Canario-Americano&action=edit&redlink=1 "Centro de Documentación Canario-Americano \(page does not exist\)") (CEDOCAM, Center for Canarian-American Documentation), located in La Laguna has a mission of strengthening cultural relations and elements of common identity between the Canaries and the Americas, through such means as conservation, information and diffusion of their shared documentary patrimony.
  * The [Centro de Fotografía Isla de Tenerife](/wiki/Centro_de_Fotograf%C3%ADa_Isla_de_Tenerife "Centro de Fotografía Isla de Tenerife") ("Island of Tenerife Photographic Center") located in Santa Cruz de Tenerife offers an annual program of expositions that allows contact with tendencies and works of various renowned and emergent photographers of the Canaries. In the future, this center will share a headquarters with the Instituto Óscar Domínguez de Arte y Cultura Contemporánea ([Óscar Domínguez Institute of Art and Culture](/w/index.php?title=%C3%93scar_Dom%C3%ADnguez_Institute_of_Art_and_Culture&action=edit&redlink=1 "Óscar Domínguez Institute of Art and Culture \(page does not exist\)")).
  * The [Tenerife Espacio de las Artes](/wiki/Tenerife_Espacio_de_las_Artes "Tenerife Espacio de las Artes") (TEA, "Tenerife Arts Space") also in Santa Cruz de Tenerife was founded to promote knowledge of the many contemporary tendencies in art and culture among the local population and visitors, by organizing cultural, scientific, educational and technical activities.



Independent of the Organismo Autónomo de Museos y Centros are: 

  * The [Casa del Carnaval](/w/index.php?title=Casa_del_Carnaval_\(Santa_Cruz_de_Tenerife\)&action=edit&redlink=1 "Casa del Carnaval \(Santa Cruz de Tenerife\) \(page does not exist\)") or _Carnival House_ , located in Santa Cruz de Tenerife, is a museum dedicated to the history of the [Carnival of Santa Cruz de Tenerife](/wiki/Carnival_of_Santa_Cruz_de_Tenerife "Carnival of Santa Cruz de Tenerife"). The enclosure has two exhibition areas; one for temporary exhibitions and also used as an assembly hall, and another for permanent exhibitions in which the costumes of the queen of the carnival of each year stand out, the original posters of the carnival, thematic videos of the history of the party, touch screens and virtual reality glasses, etc.
  * The [Municipal Museum of Fine Arts](/wiki/Museo_Municipal_de_Bellas_Artes_de_Santa_Cruz_de_Tenerife "Museo Municipal de Bellas Artes de Santa Cruz de Tenerife") in the Tenerifan capital has a permanent exhibit of the paintings and sculptures of [José de Ribera](/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_de_Ribera "José de Ribera"), [Federico Madrazo](/wiki/Federico_Madrazo "Federico Madrazo"), [Joaquín Sorolla](/wiki/Joaqu%C3%ADn_Sorolla "Joaquín Sorolla") and such Canarian artists as [Manolo Millares](/wiki/Manolo_Millares "Manolo Millares") and [Óscar Domínguez](/wiki/%C3%93scar_Dom%C3%ADnguez "Óscar Domínguez").
  * The Casa del Vino-La Baranda ("House of Wine-La Baranda"), a member of the _[Asociación de Museos del Vino de España](/w/index.php?title=Asociaci%C3%B3n_de_Museos_del_Vino_de_Espa%C3%B1a&action=edit&redlink=1 "Asociación de Museos del Vino de España \(page does not exist\)")_ (Association of Wine Museums of Spain),[177] is located in the municipality of [El Sauzal](/wiki/El_Sauzal "El Sauzal"). Its facilities include a rustic, historic _hacienda_ , a museum of the history of [viticulture](/wiki/Viticulture "Viticulture") in Tenerife, a restaurant serving typical Tenerifan food, a wine store, an audiovisual hall, and a tasting room.
  * The Casa de la Miel ("House of Honey") is an annex to the Casa del Vino-La Baranda, and was established by the _Cabildo Insular_ to support and develop the [apicultural](/wiki/Apiculture "Apiculture") (bee-keeping) sector on Tenerife. The visitor center of the Casa de la Miel offers exhibits about the history of this industry on the island and how apiculture is conducted, as well as information services and opportunities to taste Tenerifan _[denominación de origen](/wiki/Denominaci%C3%B3n_de_origen "Denominación de origen")_ honeys.[178]
  * The Museum of Iberoamerican Artisanship is located in the old convent of San Benito Abad, in La Orotava. _El centro se encuadra dentro del programa de divulgación que ejecuta el_ Center for Documentation of Artisanship in Spain and the Americas,[179] The Foundation is financed by the Ministry of Industry, Commerce and Tourism; the [Agencia Española de Cooperación Internacional](/w/index.php?title=Agencia_Espa%C3%B1ola_de_Cooperaci%C3%B3n_Internacional&action=edit&redlink=1 "Agencia Española de Cooperación Internacional \(page does not exist\)") (Spanish Agency of International Cooperation), the Comisión Nacional "Quinto Centeneario" ("Fifth Centenary" National Commission), the Consejería de Industria y Comercio del [Gobierno de Canarias](/w/index.php?title=Gobierno_de_Canarias&action=edit&redlink=1 "Gobierno de Canarias \(page does not exist\)") (Council of Industry and Commerce of the Government of the Canaries), and the [Cabildo Insular de Tenerife](/wiki/Cabildo_Insular_de_Tenerife "Cabildo Insular de Tenerife"). It has five galleries, specialized in popular musical instruments, textiles / new designs in artisanship, ceramics, fibers, and popular art.
  * The [Archaeological Museum of Puerto de la Cruz](/wiki/Archaeological_Museum_of_Puerto_de_la_Cruz "Archaeological Museum of Puerto de la Cruz") in the city of the same name is located in a traditional _casona_ (a type of house dating from the 18th–19th century), offers an archival collection comprising more than 2,600 specimens of items from the Guanche culture, and a document collection named after researcher Luis Diego Cuscoy.[180]
  * The [Regional Military Museum of the Canaries](/w/index.php?title=Regional_Military_Museum_of_the_Canaries&action=edit&redlink=1 "Regional Military Museum of the Canaries \(page does not exist\)"), is located in Santa Cruz de Tenerife, more specifically in the [Fuerte de Almeyda](/w/index.php?title=Fuerte_de_Almeyda&action=edit&redlink=1 "Fuerte de Almeyda \(page does not exist\)") district. Its galleries present all of the military history of the de Canaries, including the repelling of the attack by British Admiral [Horatio Nelson](/wiki/Horatio_Nelson "Horatio Nelson"), as well as other events and battles waged in the islands. Separate from the Regional Military Museum are files providing the Intermediate Military Archive of the Canaries and the Military Library of the Canary Islands.[181]



## Media

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=61 "Edit section: Media")]

| The examples and perspective in this section **may not represent a[worldwide view](/wiki/Wikipedia:WikiProject_Countering_systemic_bias "Wikipedia:WikiProject Countering systemic bias") of the subject**. You may [improve this section](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit), discuss the issue on the [talk page](/wiki/Talk:Tenerife "Talk:Tenerife"), or create a new section, as appropriate. _( November 2019)__([Learn how and when to remove this message](/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal "Help:Maintenance template removal"))_  
---|---  
  
Along with many Spanish-language radio and TV stations, Tenerife has two official English-language radio stations. [Coast FM](/wiki/Coast_FM_\(Tenerife\) "Coast FM \(Tenerife\)") broadcasts a mix of adult contemporary music and is the only local news service to broadcast in English. As the larger of the two stations, Coast FM can be heard across Tenerife and much of the Canary Islands from its transmitters on 106.6, 92.2 and 89.4. Energy FM is a non-stop music station that also broadcasts local news and information on the hour. 

## Transport and communications

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=62 "Edit section: Transport and communications")]

[](/wiki/File:SantaCruzHarbour.jpg)Santa Cruz de Tenerife

The island of Tenerife is served by [Tenerife North Airport](/wiki/Tenerife_North_Airport "Tenerife North Airport") (GCXO) and [Tenerife South Airport](/wiki/Tenerife_South_Airport "Tenerife South Airport") (GCTS). Thanks to its two airports, Tenerife gathers the highest passenger movement of all the [Canary Islands](/wiki/Canary_Islands "Canary Islands"), with 18,457,794 passengers.[182]

Tenerife North Airport, the smaller of the two, is located near the metropolitan area of Santa Cruz-La Laguna (423,000 inhabitants). It serves inter-insular flights as well as national and European flights. Tenerife South Airport (south) is the busiest airport in Tenerife, ranking 7th in Spain. It typically serves the mass of regular and vacation charter flights constantly arriving from most of Europe. 

The other way to arrive on Tenerife is by ferry, either to Santa Cruz de Tenerife or Los Cristianos, near Playa de Las Américas. 

A network consisting of two fast, toll-free motorways (TF1 and TF5) encircles nearly the entire island, linking all the main towns and resorts with the metropolitan area. The exception is in the West, from Adeje to Icod de los Vinos, which is traversed by a smaller winding mountain road. However, plans are in progress to complete the motorway, which caused a heavy debate between the environmentalists and the local businessmen. 

Away from the major motorways, there is a network of secondary and communal roads, varying from wide to steep, winding narrow roads, mainly unlit and often with drops on either side of the main carriageway surface. 

[](/wiki/File:Teno_Cape.JPG)Teno, the westernmost point in the island

Public transport on the island is provided by an extensive network of buses and run by [TITSA](/wiki/TITSA "TITSA"), which operates a fleet of modern, air-conditioned buses.[183] TITSA buses cover most of the island and they are fairly frequent. For more than one journey, customers can purchase a TenMas [contactless smart card](/wiki/Contactless_smart_card "Contactless smart card") for €2, which can be topped up with up to €100 travel credit. Using the TenMas card provides a discount over cash fares, and, for Tenerife residents, a card allowing unlimited travel for a monthly fee is also available.[184] The card can be purchased and topped up at bus stations and many newsagents. It is also valid on the tram in the capital, Santa Cruz. 

A hire car is sometimes a good option for discovering the remote wilderness regions, although TITSA operates reliable bus services in the remotest spots, such as the Teno Massif via Masca (355), and up the Anaga mountains (247). TITSA operates two daily services up Mount Teide – from Puerto de la Cruz (348) and from Los Christianos/Las Americas (342) up to the Teide Parador, Teleferico cable car, Montana Blanca and El Portillo. 

The metropolitan area formed by Santa Cruz and La Laguna is served by the _Tranvía de Tenerife_ (_[Tenerife Tram](/wiki/Tenerife_Tram "Tenerife Tram")_) which opened in early 2007, after 3 years of intensive works. The fairly lengthy line from Santa Cruz up the hill to La Laguna serves almost 20 stops. A second line within La Laguna was added in 2009. 

### Roads

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=63 "Edit section: Roads")]

[](/wiki/File:AutopistaTF5.jpg)TF5 motorway approaching Santa Cruz

The main means of transportation in Tenerife is by highways. The most important of these are the [Autopista del Sur](/wiki/Autopista_TF-1 "Autopista TF-1") and the [Autopista del Norte](/wiki/Autopista_TF-5 "Autopista TF-5") (the North and South Motorways), which run from the metropolitan zone to the south and north, respectively. These two motorways are connected by means of the [Autovía de Interconexión Norte-Sur](/w/index.php?title=Autov%C3%ADa_Interconexi%C3%B3n_Norte-Sur&action=edit&redlink=1 "Autovía Interconexión Norte-Sur \(page does not exist\)") in the outskirts of the metropolitan zone. Within the network of roads on the island of Tenerife, there are other minor roads that used to include the highway from San Andres and Santa Cruz.[185]

Also planned is the construction of a bypass road north of the metropolitan area of Santa Cruz de Tenerife, La Laguna. This aims to provide dual cores to [Guamasa](/w/index.php?title=Guamasa&action=edit&redlink=1 "Guamasa \(page does not exist\)") and [Acorán](/w/index.php?title=Acor%C3%A1n&action=edit&redlink=1 "Acorán \(page does not exist\)"), by way of [Los Baldíos](/w/index.php?title=Los_Bald%C3%ADos_\(Tenerife\)&action=edit&redlink=1 "Los Baldíos \(Tenerife\) \(page does not exist\)"), [Centenero](/w/index.php?title=Centenero_\(Tenerife\)&action=edit&redlink=1 "Centenero \(Tenerife\) \(page does not exist\)"), [Llano del Moro](/w/index.php?title=Llano_del_Moro&action=edit&redlink=1 "Llano del Moro \(page does not exist\)"), [El Sobradillo](/w/index.php?title=El_Sobradillo&action=edit&redlink=1 "El Sobradillo \(page does not exist\)"), [El Tablero](/w/index.php?title=El_Tablero&action=edit&redlink=1 "El Tablero \(page does not exist\)"), and [El Chorrillo](/w/index.php?title=El_Chorrillo_\(Tenerife\)&action=edit&redlink=1 "El Chorrillo \(Tenerife\) \(page does not exist\)"), among other neighbourhoods. The route will be approximately 20 kilometres (12 mi) long and will cost an estimated 190 million euros (270 million in American dollars).[186]

### Airports

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=64 "Edit section: Airports")]

[](/wiki/File:Rodeos_verde.JPG)Tenerife North Airport

Tenerife is most easily reached by air. There are two airports: [Tenerife South Airport](/wiki/Tenerife_South_Airport "Tenerife South Airport"), in the south, and [Tenerife North Airport](/wiki/Tenerife_North_Airport "Tenerife North Airport"), near Santa Cruz. Overall, Tenerife has the highest annual passenger count and the greatest number of arrivals in Canary Islands, made more popular by the frequency of cheap flights from many European destinations. Tenerife North Airport and Tenerife South Airport together account for the highest passenger numbers in the Islands with some 14 million passengers annually (AENA report[187]). Of the two airports on the island, Tenerife South is the most popular tourist destination.[188][189]

### Ports

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=65 "Edit section: Ports")]

Besides air transport, Tenerife has two principal maritime ports: the [Port of Santa Cruz de Tenerife](/wiki/Port_of_Santa_Cruz_de_Tenerife "Port of Santa Cruz de Tenerife") (_Puerto de Santa Cruz_), which serves the various capitals of the Canary Islands, especially those in the east; and the [Port of Los Cristianos](/wiki/Port_of_Los_Cristianos "Port of Los Cristianos") (_Puerto de Los Cristianos_), which serves the various island capitals of the province of Santa Cruz de Tenerife. The first port also has passenger services, which connect with the mainland port of [Cádiz](/wiki/C%C3%A1diz "Cádiz") (and vice versa). In 2017, a large important port was opened in the south of the island, the [Port of Granadilla](/wiki/Port_of_Granadilla "Port of Granadilla"), and another one is planned in the west, in [Fonsalía](/w/index.php?title=Port_of_Fonsal%C3%ADa&action=edit&redlink=1 "Port of Fonsalía \(page does not exist\)").[190] The Port of Santa Cruz de Tenerife is the first fishing port in the Canary Islands with approximately 7,500 tons of fish caught, according to the Statistical Yearbook of the State Ports 2006 (the latest of which is changing). Following this report is the largest port number of passengers recorded. Similarly, the second port of Spain moving ship and loaded into cars, only surpassed by the [Port of Algeciras](/wiki/Port_of_Algeciras "Port of Algeciras") Bay.[191] The port's facilities include a border inspection post (BIP) approved by the European Union, which is responsible for inspecting all types of imports from third countries or exports to countries outside the European Economic Area. 

### Buses (_guaguas_)

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=66 "Edit section: Buses \(guaguas\)")]

Tenerife has an extensive system of buses, which are called _guaguas_ in the Canary Islands. The bus system is used both within the cities and also connects most of the towns and cities of the island. There are bus stations in all of the major towns, such as the [Intercambiador de Transportes de Santa Cruz de Tenerife](/wiki/Intercambiador "Intercambiador"). 

### Taxis

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=67 "Edit section: Taxis")]

There is a well-regulated taxi service on the island.[_[citation needed](/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_]

### Tramway

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=68 "Edit section: Tramway")]

[](/wiki/File:Tranv%C3%ADa_de_Tenerife1.jpg)Tramway servicing between Santa Cruz and La Laguna

Since 2007, the [Tenerife Tram](/wiki/Tenerife_Tram "Tenerife Tram") connects [Santa Cruz de Tenerife](/wiki/Santa_Cruz_de_Tenerife "Santa Cruz de Tenerife") and [La Laguna](/wiki/San_Crist%C3%B3bal_de_La_Laguna "San Cristóbal de La Laguna") through the suburb of Taco. There are 20 stops and it covers a distance of 12.5 km (7.8 mi) in 37 minutes. It calls at some points of interest including Tenerife's two major hospitals, the university complex of Guajara, and a number of museums and theatres. Concerning its power supply, it will support development of further wind farms to provide it with 100 percent clean energy.[192]

#### Railway plans

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=69 "Edit section: Railway plans")]

Main article: [Tren del Sur](/wiki/Tren_del_Sur "Tren del Sur")

By 2005, plans for a light-rail network linking the capital with the South had been approved by both the [Tenerife Council](/wiki/Cabildo_Canaries "Cabildo Canaries") and the Canary Islands Government, though the discussion with the central Spanish Government stalled on budget issues.[193] The original intent was to establish two railway systems that would serve the northern and southern sides of the island connecting these with the capital.[194] By March 2011, these intentions had been replaced by advanced plans for a single 80 km (50 mi) [high-speed rail](/wiki/High-speed_rail "High-speed rail") line, the "[South Train](/wiki/Tren_del_Sur "Tren del Sur")" which would connect [Santa Cruz de Tenerife](/wiki/Santa_Cruz_de_Tenerife "Santa Cruz de Tenerife") with [Adeje](/wiki/Adeje "Adeje") via Santa Maria de Añaza, [Candelaria](/wiki/Candelaria,_Tenerife "Candelaria, Tenerife"), San Isidro, [Tenerife South Airport](/wiki/Tenerife_South_Airport "Tenerife South Airport"), and a main stopover station at [Adeje](/wiki/Adeje "Adeje") which would be designed to service up to 25,000 passengers per day. Trains would run every 15 minutes during rush hours, and would achieve speeds up to 220 km/h (137 mph). The project, which involves 9 tunnels, 12 false tunnels (together 22.1 km) and 33 viaducts (8.3 km) has been budgeted at EUR 1.7 bn. It has met staunch opposition from local environmentalists. 

Plans for the train system were resurrected in 2017. Construction is expected to start by 2027.[195]

An alternate plan for a high-speed [Transrapid maglev](/wiki/Transrapid#Tenerife "Transrapid") has also been put forward.[196][197]

### Tourist bus train

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=70 "Edit section: Tourist bus train")]

A [tourist train](/wiki/Trackless_train "Trackless train") (Tren Turístico) serves Costa Adeje to Los Cristianos with several stops including in Playa de las Americas. It is served by a motor vehicle designed to look like a train, with separate carriage attached to it.

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=71 "Edit section: Cableway")]

[](/wiki/File:Cabina_Telef%C3%A9rico.jpg)Teide cableway
