## Sports

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=72 "Edit section: Sports")]

Main article: [Sport in Tenerife](/wiki/Sport_in_Tenerife "Sport in Tenerife")

On the island of Tenerife, a large number of sports are practised, both outdoors and indoors in the various facilities available throughout the island. The sports are numerous – Diving, Rock Climbing, Walking, Cycling, Sailing, Golf, Surfing, Go-Karting, Paragliding[198] – the all year round weather makes it ideal for a wide variety of outdoor sports. There are also many indoor sporting facilities including fully equipped including 'Tenerife Top Training' centre in Adeje on the South of the Island. Its most well-known sports team is [football](/wiki/Association_football "Association football") club [CD Tenerife](/wiki/CD_Tenerife "CD Tenerife") based in Santa Cruz. The club has spent time in the Spanish top flight, but have in recent decades primarily played in the second division of Spanish football. Also worth mentioning is the [ultramarathon](/wiki/Ultramarathon "Ultramarathon") [CajaMar Tenerife Bluetrail](/wiki/CajaMar_Tenerife_Bluetrail "CajaMar Tenerife Bluetrail"), the highest race in Spain and second in Europe,[199] with the participation of several countries and great international repercussions. 

## Healthcare

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=73 "Edit section: Healthcare")]

[](/wiki/File:Hospital_Universitario_de_Canarias.png)[Hospital Universitario de Canarias](/wiki/Hospital_Universitario_de_Canarias "Hospital Universitario de Canarias")

The main hospitals on the island are the [Hospital Universitario de Canarias](/wiki/Hospital_Universitario_de_Canarias "Hospital Universitario de Canarias") and the [Hospital Universitario Nuestra Señora de Candelaria](/wiki/Hospital_Universitario_Nuestra_Se%C3%B1ora_de_Candelaria "Hospital Universitario Nuestra Señora de Candelaria"). Both are third-level hospitals, with specialist facilities that serve all of the Canary Islands.[200] They are both affiliated with the education and research network of the [Universidad de La Laguna](/wiki/Universidad_de_La_Laguna "Universidad de La Laguna"). However, they belong to different bodies, since the first one is under the directives of the [Servicio Canario de la Salud](/w/index.php?title=Servicio_Canario_de_la_Salud&action=edit&redlink=1 "Servicio Canario de la Salud \(page does not exist\)") (Canarian Health Service).[201][202] The Hospital Universitario Nuestra Señora de Candelaria it is the largest hospital complex in the Canary Islands.[203]

In addition, two large new hospitals have recently been built in the north and south areas of the island, located in the municipalities of [Icod de los Vinos](/wiki/Icod_de_los_Vinos "Icod de los Vinos") and [Arona](/wiki/Arona,_Tenerife "Arona, Tenerife") respectively. The [Hospital del Norte de Tenerife](/wiki/Hospital_del_Norte_de_Tenerife "Hospital del Norte de Tenerife") (_Tenerife North Hospital_) opened in 2012 and the [Hospital del Sur de Tenerife](/wiki/Hospital_del_Sur_de_Tenerife "Hospital del Sur de Tenerife") (_Tenerife South Hospital_) opened in 2015.[204] These centers will function, according to their classification, as second level hospitals, with services of hospitalization, advanced diagnosis, ambulances and emergencies, and rehabilitation, etc. There are also a total of 39 centers of primary care and specialized clinics which complete the sanitary infrastructure of Tenerife.[205]

## Gastronomy

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=74 "Edit section: Gastronomy")]

### Fish

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=75 "Edit section: Fish")]

As an island, Tenerife enjoys an abundance of fish of various kinds. The species that are consumed the most are the [Combtooth blennies](/wiki/Combtooth_blennies "Combtooth blennies") (_viejas_), as well as [sea bream](/wiki/Sea_bream "Sea bream") (_sama_), [red porgy](/wiki/Red_porgy "Red porgy") (_bocinegro_), [gold lined bream](/wiki/Sarpa_salpa "Sarpa salpa") (_salema_), [grouper](/wiki/Grouper "Grouper") (_mero_), and various and abundant types of _[Thunnus](/wiki/Thunnus "Thunnus")_. The [Atlantic mackerel](/wiki/Atlantic_mackerel "Atlantic mackerel") (_caballa_), [sardine](/wiki/Sardine "Sardine") (_sardine_), and [Jack mackerels](/wiki/Jack_mackerels "Jack mackerels") (_chicharros_) are also consumed frequently. [Moray eels](/wiki/Moray_eels "Moray eels") (_morenas_) are also eaten, usually fried. Most seafood is cooked simply, usually boiled, or prepared "a la espalda" (cut into two equally shaped pieces along the spine) or "a la sal" (baked in salt). These dishes are usually accompanied by _mojo_ (a local sauce) and wrinkly potatoes.[33][53]

### Meat

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=76 "Edit section: Meat")]

The typical festive meat dish of marinated pork [tacos](/wiki/Taco "Taco") is a very popular dish prepared for town festivities in _ventorrillos_ , bars and private homes.[206] Rabbit in [salmorejo](/wiki/Salmorejo "Salmorejo"), goat, beef, pork and poultry are regularly consumed.[33][53]

### Canarian wrinkly potatoes

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=77 "Edit section: Canarian wrinkly potatoes")]

[](/wiki/File:Papasarrugadas.jpg)Canarian wrinkly potatoes, with red mojo

The fish dishes along with the meats are often accompanied by [wrinkly potatoes](/wiki/Canarian_wrinkly_potatoes "Canarian wrinkly potatoes") (_papas arrugadas_). This is a typical Canarian dish which simply refers to the way the cooked potatoes look. They are boiled in their skins, in water with much salt, and the water is allowed to evaporate, leaving a salty crust.[33][53]

### Mojos

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=78 "Edit section: Mojos")]

[Mojo](/wiki/Mojo_\(sauce\) "Mojo \(sauce\)"), a word probably of Portuguese origin, describes a typical Canarian sauce, served as an accompaniment to food. The sauces come in a variety of colours, flavours and textures, and are usually served cold, often in separate dishes, for the diner to choose how much to apply. Green mojo usually includes coriander, parsley, and garlic; whilst red mojo is piquant, and made from a mix of hot and sweet peppers. A wide variety of other ingredients are also used, including; almonds, cheese, saffron and fried bread.[33][53] Mojos are served with most meat, and some fish, dishes, and are often used on potatoes, or bread is dipped into them. 

### Cheeses

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=79 "Edit section: Cheeses")]

Tenerife exports about 3,400 tons of cheese per year, representing about 50 percent of the output of the island, and about 25 percent of the entire Canary Islands.[_[citation needed](/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_]

After the conquest of the Canary Islands, one of the first commercial activities to be started was cheese production. The sale of cheese provided the inhabitants with an income and cheese was even used as a form of currency for exchange and sale, becoming a crucial product in agricultural areas of the island. 

Cheese grew to become one of the most commonly produced and consumed products on the island and is regularly served as part of a starter course or as a snack. Farms at Arico, La Orotava and Teno produced a variety of cheeses, including soft cheeses, cured, smoked and were mostly handmade. Today the main product is [goat cheese](/wiki/Goat_cheese "Goat cheese"), although certain amounts are made from sheep's or cow's milk, and according to the _Registro General Sanitario de Alimentos_ , the general health registry, around 75 different [cottage cheeses](/wiki/Cottage_cheese "Cottage cheese") are produced.[207] The cheeses of the Canaries have generally received good international reviews, noted for their sweetness which differentiates them from certain other European cheeses.[33][53][208] In particular, Tenerifan cured goats cheese was awarded best cheese in the world final of the 2008 [World Cheese Awards](/wiki/World_Cheese_Awards "World Cheese Awards") held in [Dublin](/wiki/Dublin "Dublin"), Ireland.[209]

Cheeses from Tenerife now have a quality mark promoted by the [Fundación Tenerife Rural](/w/index.php?title=Fundaci%C3%B3n_Tenerife_Rural&action=edit&redlink=1 "Fundación Tenerife Rural \(page does not exist\)"), to standardize their quality in an attempt to publicize the qualities of the cheese and improve its marketing.[207]

### Gofio

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=80 "Edit section: Gofio")]

[](/wiki/File:Gofioescaldado.jpg)Gofio escaldado

[Gofio](/wiki/Gofio "Gofio") is one of the more traditional elements of cooking on the island, It is made with [cereal grains](/wiki/Cereal_grain "Cereal grain") that are roasted and then ground. Increasingly used to make a gofio on the island is wheat although there are other types, and they are often made with [chickpeas](/wiki/Chickpea "Chickpea"). Relatively common is a mixed-type with wheat. It was served as main food to the _guanches_ even before the Spanish conquest. In later times of scarcity or famine it was a staple of the popular Canarian diet. Today it is eaten as a main dish (gofio escaldado) or an accompaniment to different dishes, meats, fishes, soups, desserts. Some famous cooks have even made gofio ice cream, receiving good comments from the critics.[33][53]

### Confectionery

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=81 "Edit section: Confectionery")]

Confectionery in Tenerife is represented and strongly influenced by La Palma, with confections like _bienmesabe_ , _leche asada_ , _Príncipe Alberto_ , _[frangollo](/wiki/Frangollo "Frangollo")_ , _huevos moles_ , and _quesillo_.[33][53]

### Wines

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=82 "Edit section: Wines")]

[Viticulture](/wiki/Viticulture "Viticulture") in the archipelago, and especially in Tenerife dates back to the conquest, when the settlers brought a variety of vines to plant. In the 16th and 17th centuries, wine production played an important role in the economy, and many families were dedicated to the culture and business. Of special mention is _malvasía canary_ , considered the best wine of Tenerife and at the time one of the most desired wines in the world, being shipped across to the major warehouses of Europe and America.[210] Writers such as [William Shakespeare](/wiki/William_Shakespeare "William Shakespeare") and [Walter Scott](/wiki/Walter_Scott "Walter Scott") make reference to the wine in some of their works.[211] Tenerife has 5 main wine growing regions. These include [Abona](/wiki/Abona_\(DO\) "Abona \(DO\)"), [Valle de Güímar](/wiki/Valle_de_G%C3%BC%C3%ADmar "Valle de Güímar"), [Valle de La Orotava](/wiki/Valle_de_La_Orotava "Valle de La Orotava"), [Tacoronte-Acentejo](/wiki/Tacoronte-Acentejo "Tacoronte-Acentejo") and [Ycoden-Daute-Isora](/wiki/Ycoden-Daute-Isora "Ycoden-Daute-Isora").[212]

This typical gastronomy is served in popular establishments known as _[guachinches](/wiki/Guachinche "Guachinche")_ , opening day is the day of _San Andrés_ , 30 November, also known as the young wine festival _Festival de Vino Joven_. The wine of the new harvest is traditionally served with roasted chestnuts, maturing at the same time, and grilled sardines, thus the season normally lasts from late autumn until early spring. 

## In popular culture

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=83 "Edit section: In popular culture")]

| This section **may contain[irrelevant](/wiki/Wikipedia:What_Wikipedia_is_not#Wikipedia_is_not_an_indiscriminate_collection_of_information "Wikipedia:What Wikipedia is not") references to [popular culture](/wiki/Wikipedia:Manual_of_Style/Trivia_sections#"In_popular_culture"_and_"Cultural_references"_material "Wikipedia:Manual of Style/Trivia sections")**. Please help [improve it](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit) by removing such content and adding [citations](/wiki/Wikipedia:Citing_sources "Wikipedia:Citing sources") to [reliable](/wiki/Wikipedia:Reliable_sources "Wikipedia:Reliable sources"), [independent sources](/wiki/Wikipedia:Independent_sources "Wikipedia:Independent sources"). _( January 2023)_

### Cities

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=84 "Edit section: Cities")]

The city of [Brisbane](/wiki/Brisbane "Brisbane") in Queensland, Australia, has a historical inner suburb named [Teneriffe](/wiki/Teneriffe,_Queensland "Teneriffe, Queensland"), named by one of the first European landowners in the area, James Gibbon, because it reminded him of [Mount Teide](/wiki/Teide "Teide").[213]

Other cities in the world whose name has its origins in the island are: [Tenerife, Magdalena](/wiki/Tenerife,_Magdalena "Tenerife, Magdalena"), a town and municipality in Colombia; [San Carlos de Tenerife](/wiki/San_Carlos_de_Tenerife "San Carlos de Tenerife"), a city in the Dominican Republic.[214]

### Cinema

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=85 "Edit section: Cinema")]

Over the last few years,[_[clarification needed](/wiki/Wikipedia:Please_clarify "Wikipedia:Please clarify")_] Tenerife has become a popular filming location, being featured in several Hollywood blockbusters.[215] Some of the most important films made on the island are: 

  * _[One Million Years B.C.](/wiki/One_Million_Years_B.C. "One Million Years B.C.")_ : British film of 1966 directed by [Don Chaffey](/wiki/Don_Chaffey "Don Chaffey") and shot in Teide National Park.[216]
  * _[Clash of the Titans](/wiki/Clash_of_the_Titans_\(2010_film\) "Clash of the Titans \(2010 film\)")_ : American film of 2010, directed by [Louis Leterrier](/wiki/Louis_Leterrier "Louis Leterrier"). It is mainly located in different locations in Tenerife, such as [Teide National Park](/wiki/Teide_National_Park "Teide National Park"), [Icod de los Vinos](/wiki/Icod_de_los_Vinos "Icod de los Vinos") and [Buenavista del Norte](/wiki/Buenavista_del_Norte "Buenavista del Norte"), and [Chío](/w/index.php?title=Ch%C3%ADo&action=edit&redlink=1 "Chío \(page does not exist\)") pine forests in the municipality of [Guía de Isora](/wiki/Gu%C3%ADa_de_Isora "Guía de Isora").[217]
  * _[Breaking Bad](/wiki/Breaking_Bad "Breaking Bad")_ : In the American television series Season 3, episode 1 _[No Más (Breaking Bad)](/wiki/No_M%C3%A1s_\(Breaking_Bad\) "No Más \(Breaking Bad\)")_ of 2010, Walter White refers to the air crash of Tenerife while speaking at the school program.[218][_[circular reference](/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Wikipedia_and_sources_that_mirror_or_use_it "Wikipedia:Verifiability")_]
  * _[Wrath of the Titans](/wiki/Wrath_of_the_Titans "Wrath of the Titans")_ : American film 2012, directed by [Jonathan Liebesman](/wiki/Jonathan_Liebesman "Jonathan Liebesman"). Shot largely in the Teide National Park.[219]
  * _[1898, Our Last Men in the Philippines](/wiki/1898,_Our_Last_Men_in_the_Philippines "1898, Our Last Men in the Philippines")_ : Spanish film of 2012 directed by [Salvador Calvo](/wiki/Salvador_Calvo "Salvador Calvo"), with some scenes shot in Tenerife.[220]
  * _[Fast& Furious 6](/wiki/Fast_%26_Furious_6 "Fast & Furious 6")_: 2013 American film directed by [Justin Lin](/wiki/Justin_Lin "Justin Lin"). In the beginning of the movie, Dom along with Brian, Mia, and their son, live in Tenerife after their heist in Brazil in Fast Five. Also, the tank chase scene was filmed on part of the [Autopista TF-1](/wiki/Autopista_TF-1 "Autopista TF-1").[_[citation needed](/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_]
  * _[Jason Bourne](/wiki/Jason_Bourne_\(film\) "Jason Bourne \(film\)")_ : American film of 2015, directed by [Paul Greengrass](/wiki/Paul_Greengrass "Paul Greengrass") and shot in [Santa Cruz de Tenerife](/wiki/Santa_Cruz_de_Tenerife "Santa Cruz de Tenerife"). For this, the city was specially set to simulate the Greek cities of [Athens](/wiki/Athens "Athens") and [Piraeus](/wiki/Piraeus "Piraeus"). The [Plaza de España](/wiki/Plaza_de_Espa%C3%B1a_\(Santa_Cruz_de_Tenerife\) "Plaza de España \(Santa Cruz de Tenerife\)"), which is the main square in the capital of Tenerife, was decorated to represent the [Syntagma Square](/wiki/Syntagma_Square "Syntagma Square").[221]
  * _[Rambo: Last Blood](/wiki/Rambo:_Last_Blood "Rambo: Last Blood")_ : American film of 2019, directed by [Adrian Grunberg](/wiki/Adrian_Grunberg "Adrian Grunberg") and with the performances of [Sylvester Stallone](/wiki/Sylvester_Stallone "Sylvester Stallone"), [Matt Cirulnick](/w/index.php?title=Matt_Cirulnick&action=edit&redlink=1 "Matt Cirulnick \(page does not exist\)") and [Paz Vega](/wiki/Paz_Vega "Paz Vega"), among others. It was shot in different locations on the island, such as: different neighborhoods of Santa Cruz de Tenerife and [San Cristóbal de La Laguna](/wiki/San_Crist%C3%B3bal_de_La_Laguna "San Cristóbal de La Laguna"), areas of [Puerto de la Cruz](/wiki/Puerto_de_la_Cruz "Puerto de la Cruz"), [Santa Úrsula](/wiki/Santa_%C3%9Arsula "Santa Úrsula"), [Arico](/wiki/Arico "Arico") and the roads around the Teide National Park, among others places.[222]
  * _[Den of Thieves 2: Pantera](/wiki/Den_of_Thieves_2:_Pantera "Den of Thieves 2: Pantera")_ : American film of 2025, directed by [Christian Gudegast](/wiki/Christian_Gudegast "Christian Gudegast"), was largely shot in Santa Cruz de Tenerife.[223]



### Music

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=86 "Edit section: Music")]

Likewise, the island has been home to several musical recordings and as a scenic setting for music videos: 

  * "[Tie Your Mother Down](/wiki/Tie_Your_Mother_Down "Tie Your Mother Down")": song of the English musician [Brian May](/wiki/Brian_May "Brian May"), who studied different astronomical phenomena from [Izaña](/w/index.php?title=Iza%C3%B1a&action=edit&redlink=1 "Izaña \(page does not exist\)") in the Cañadas del Teide and wrote this song in this national park.[224]
  * [Mike Oldfield](/wiki/Mike_Oldfield "Mike Oldfield") included in his compilation _The Complete fel_ of 1985, the song _Mount Teide_ dedicated to this volcano from Tenerife.[_[citation needed](/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_]
  * The music video of song "[If I Let You Go](/wiki/If_I_Let_You_Go "If I Let You Go")", filmed on location in Tenerife, depicts Irish boyband [Westlife](/wiki/Westlife "Westlife") walking along a beach and singing by a hillside surrounded by red and yellow flags.[_[citation needed](/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_]
  * "[The Island – Pt. 1 Dawn](/wiki/The_Island_\(Pendulum_song\) "The Island \(Pendulum song\)")": video clip of the Australian group [Pendulum](/wiki/Pendulum_\(drum_and_bass_band\) "Pendulum \(drum and bass band\)") which was recorded entirely in Teide National Park and published in 2010. In this video you can distinguish at the beginning of the video the silhouette of the Teide volcano and the characteristic landscapes of the Park During the entire filming.
  * Songwriter and singer [Ed Sheeran](/wiki/Ed_Sheeran "Ed Sheeran") wrote a song, "Tenerife Sea", for his [second studio album](/wiki/X_\(Ed_Sheeran_album\) "X \(Ed Sheeran album\)") _X_ , that mentions the island entitled [Tenerife Sea](/wiki/Tenerife_Sea "Tenerife Sea"). The title is a reference to a line wherein Sheeran compares the color of his love interest's eyes to that of the sea surrounding the island.
  * The video clip of the song "[Do It for Your Lover](/wiki/Do_It_for_Your_Lover "Do It for Your Lover")", with which the Spanish singer [Manel Navarro](/wiki/Manel_Navarro "Manel Navarro") [represented Spain](/wiki/Spain_in_the_Eurovision_Song_Contest "Spain in the Eurovision Song Contest") in the [Eurovision Song Contest 2017](/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest "Eurovision Song Contest"), was recorded in different locations of Tenerife, especially in the [Macizo de Anaga](/wiki/Macizo_de_Anaga "Macizo de Anaga") and in El Porís ([Arico](/wiki/Arico "Arico")).[225][226]
  * Singer [Blas Cantó](/wiki/Blas_Cant%C3%B3 "Blas Cantó") (representative of Spain at the [Eurovision Song Contest 2020](/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest_2020 "Eurovision Song Contest 2020")) recorded in Tenerife and [Lanzarote](/wiki/Lanzarote "Lanzarote") the video clip of the song "[Universo](/wiki/Universo_\(song\) "Universo \(song\)")", with which he represents Spain.[227]



### Video games

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=87 "Edit section: Video games")]

  * _[Asphalt 8: Airborne](/wiki/Asphalt_8:_Airborne "Asphalt 8: Airborne")_ features four race tracks on Tenerife.[228]
  * _[Operation Flashpoint: Cold War Crisis](/wiki/Operation_Flashpoint:_Cold_War_Crisis "Operation Flashpoint: Cold War Crisis")_ houses that fictional Kolgujev island, based on Tenerife.
  * In _[My Summer Car](/wiki/My_Summer_Car "My Summer Car")_ the main character's parents go to Tenerife on vacation.



### In literature

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=88 "Edit section: In literature")]

Literary works featuring Tenerife include _[The Companion](/wiki/The_Companion_\(short_story\) "The Companion \(short story\)")_ and _[The Man from the Sea](/wiki/The_Man_from_the_Sea_\(short_story\) "The Man from the Sea \(short story\)")_ , both by [Agatha Christie](/wiki/Agatha_Christie "Agatha Christie"); _La cueva de las mil momias_ by [Alberto Vazquez-Figueroa](/wiki/Alberto_V%C3%A1zquez-Figueroa "Alberto Vázquez-Figueroa"); _[Thieves' Picnic](/wiki/Thieves%27_Picnic "Thieves' Picnic")_ by [Leslie Charteris](/wiki/Leslie_Charteris "Leslie Charteris"); _El Sarcófago de las tres llaves_ by Pompeyo Reina Moreno;[229] and _Atentado_ by Mariano Gambín.[230]

## International relations

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Tenerife&action=edit&section=89 "Edit section: International relations")]

See also: [List of twin towns and sister cities in Spain](/wiki/List_of_twin_towns_and_sister_cities_in_Spain "List of twin towns and sister cities in Spain")

Tenerife is [twinned](/wiki/Twin_towns_and_sister_cities "Twin towns and sister cities") with: 

  * [](/wiki/United_States "United States") [Miami Dade](/wiki/Miami_Dade "Miami Dade"), United States[231]
  * [](/wiki/Dominican_Republic "Dominican Republic") [Santo Domingo](/wiki/Santo_Domingo "Santo Domingo"), Dominican Republic[232]

  *[POTUS  
No.]: Number
  *[b.]: born
  *[q.v.]: see also
  *[25]: Not actually given in Africanus; the number is deduced from Manetho's other copyists, as well as Africanus' own sum of years.
  *[69 years]: The correct sum should be 97; the total excluding Queen Hatshepsut is 75 years.
  *[263 years]: This is actually the sum of the first 13 kings, which amounts to 262 years. Adding the reign of the 14th king raises the number to 267 years.
  *[348 years]: The sum of the first thirteen kings is 263 years, just as Africanus. The "correct" sum of years would be 376.
  *[41 years]: Africanus gives 46 years, but this does not match the stated total.
  *[35 years]: Africanus gives 14 years, but this does not match the stated total.
  *[ 120 years]: Africanus' sum of items comes to 130 years.
  *[40 years]: Eusebius gives 25 years
  *[8 years]: Eusebius gives 12 years
  *[ 40 years]: Eusebius gives 44 years
  *[14 years]: Eusebius gives 12 years
  *[18 years]: Eusebius gives 20 years
